    3.1  K8s Concepts You Need for GitOps
         - Pods, Deployments, Services, ConfigMaps, Secrets, Ingress
         - How these map to GitOps YAML files
         - The "desired state" concept in K8s vs GitOps
         [MAPPING TABLE: K8s objects → GitOps YAML files]

         [K8S OBJECTS → GITOPS YAML FILES]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  K8s Object       │ File in Git Repo      │ What It Does     │
         │  ─────────────────┼───────────────────────┼──────────────────│
         │  Deployment       │ deployment.yaml       │ "Run 3 easypj"   │
         │  Service          │ service.yaml          │ "Expose port 80" │
         │  ConfigMap        │ configmap.yaml        │ "Use these envs" │
         │  Secret           │ secret.yaml (SEALED!) │ "DB password"    │
         │  Ingress          │ ingress.yaml          │ "Route traffic"  │
         │  Namespace        │ namespace.yaml        │ "Isolate envs"   │
         │  PersistentVolume │ pvc.yaml              │ "Store data"     │
         │  HPA              │ hpa.yaml              │ "Auto-scale"     │
         │  NetworkPolicy    │ netpol.yaml           │ "Restrict access"│
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    3.2  Imperative vs Declarative — The GitOps Mindset
         - Imperative: "kubectl run nginx — create this now"
         - Declarative: "I want 3 nginx pods" (written in YAML)
         - Why GitOps REQUIRES declarative configuration
         [COMPARISON: Imperative commands vs Declarative YAML]

         [IMPERATIVE vs DECLARATIVE — with easypj examples]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │                                                              │
         │  ❌ IMPERATIVE (NOT GitOps-compatible)                        │
         │  $ kubectl scale deployment easypj --replicas=5              │
         │  ⚠️ Problem: Git doesn't know about this change!             │
         │  ⚠️ Next Git sync → OVERWRITES back to 3 (Git says 3)       │
         │                                                              │
         │  ✅ DECLARATIVE (GitOps way)                                  │
         │  Edit deployment.yaml: replicas: 3 → replicas: 5             │
         │  $ git commit -m "Scale easypj to 5"                         │
         │  $ git push                                                  │
         │  → GitOps agent: "Git says 5 → cluster has 3 → fixing..."   │
         │  ✅ Git = Reality. Always in sync.                            │
         │                                                              │
         │  💡 RULE: If it doesn't start with a Git commit,              │
         │     it's not GitOps!                                          │
         │                                                              │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [DECLARATIVE CONFIGURATION — the complete picture]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │                                                              │
         │  GITOPS DECLARATIVE STACK:                                    │
         │                                                              │
         │  Layer 1: Git Repository (Source of Truth)                   │
         │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐   │
         │  │ /easypj-config/                                       │   │
         │  │   ├── base/deployment.yaml     ← "I want 3 easypj"    │   │
         │  │   ├── base/service.yaml        ← "on port 8000"       │   │
         │  │   └── overlays/production/     ← "with these settings"│   │
         │  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘   │
         │         │                                                    │
         │         │ GitOps Agent (ArgoCD/Flux) reads this              │
         │         ▼                                                    │
         │  Layer 2: Kubernetes Cluster (Actual State)                  │
         │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐   │
         │  │ kubectl get all -n easypj-prod                        │   │
         │  │ NAME                    READY   STATUS                │   │
         │  │ pod/easypj-abc123       1/1     Running   ← matches!  │   │
         │  │ pod/easypj-def456       1/1     Running   ← matches!  │   │
         │  │ pod/easypj-ghi789       1/1     Running   ← matches!  │   │
         │  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘   │
         │                                                              │
         │  💡 If Layer 1 ≠ Layer 2 → GitOps agent fixes it!           │
         │                                                              │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    3.3  YAML Deep Dive for GitOps
         - YAML syntax essentials (indentation, lists, dictionaries)
         - Common YAML pitfalls in GitOps
         - YAML anchors and aliases for DRY configs
         [CODE BLOCK: Well-structured deployment.yaml with comments]

         [YAML ANATOMY — annotated deployment.yaml for easypj]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  # deployment.yaml — The heart of your GitOps config         │
         │                                                              │
         │  apiVersion: apps/v1           # ← K8s API version           │
         │  kind: Deployment              # ← Type of resource           │
         │  metadata:                                                   │
         │    name: easypj                # ← App name                   │
         │    namespace: production       # ← Where it runs              │
         │    labels:                                                   │
         │      app: easypj               # ← Labels for selection       │
         │      tier: backend                                           │
         │  spec:                                                       │
         │    replicas: 3                 # ← DESIRED STATE (Git says 3)│
         │    selector:                                                 │
         │      matchLabels:                                            │
         │        app: easypj             # ← Which pods to manage       │
         │    template:                                                 │
         │      metadata:                                               │
         │        labels:                                               │
         │          app: easypj                                         │
         │      spec:                                                   │
         │        containers:                                           │
         │        - name: easypj                                        │
         │          image: registry/easypj:v2.1.0  # ← Version in Git!  │
         │          ports:                                              │
         │          - containerPort: 8000                               │
         │          resources:                                          │
         │            requests:                                         │
         │              memory: "256Mi"                                 │
         │            limits:                                           │
         │              memory: "512Mi"                                 │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    3.4  Kustomize — Config Without Templates
         - What is Kustomize and why it's built into kubectl
         - Base + Overlays pattern for multi-environment
         - Patching, image transformers, configMapGenerator
         - Kustomize for easypj: dev, staging, production overlays
         [CODE BLOCK: Complete kustomization.yaml structure]

         [KUSTOMIZE OVERLAY PATTERN — easypj environments]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │                                                              │
         │  easypj-config/                                              │
         │  ├── base/                     ← Common config for ALL envs  │
         │  │   ├── deployment.yaml       ← Base deployment             │
         │  │   ├── service.yaml          ← Base service                │
         │  │   └── kustomization.yaml    ← Lists resources             │
         │  │                                                           │
         │  ├── overlays/                                               │
         │  │   ├── dev/                  ← Development environment     │
         │  │   │   ├── kustomization.yaml  patches replicas: 1         │
         │  │   │   └── configmap.yaml    ← Dev-specific settings       │
         │  │   │                                                       │
         │  │   ├── staging/              ← Staging (pre-prod)          │
         │  │   │   ├── kustomization.yaml  patches replicas: 2         │
         │  │   │   └── ingress.yaml      ← Staging domain              │
         │  │   │                                                       │
         │  │   └── production/           ← LIVE environment             │
         │  │       ├── kustomization.yaml  patches replicas: 5         │
         │  │       ├── configmap.yaml    ← Prod DB settings            │
         │  │       └── ingress.yaml      ← easypj.com domain           │
         │                                                              │
         │  HOW TO USE:                                                 │
         │  $ kubectl apply -k overlays/dev         # Deploy to dev     │
         │  $ kubectl apply -k overlays/production  # Deploy to prod    │
         │                                                              │
         │  In GitOps: ArgoCD/Flux points to specific overlay           │
         │                                                              │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    3.5  Helm — Package Manager for Kubernetes
         - What is Helm? Charts, values, templates, releases
         - Helm vs Kustomize — when to use which
         - Using Helm with GitOps (ArgoCD + Helm)
         - Creating a Helm chart for easypj
         [CODE BLOCK: easypj Helm chart structure]

         [HELM vs KUSTOMIZE — comparison for GitOps]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  Feature         │ Kustomize              │ Helm              │
         │  ────────────────┼────────────────────────┼───────────────────│
         │  Approach        │ Patch-based (overlay)  │ Template-based    │
         │  Learning Curve  │ 🟢 Easy (just YAML)    │ 🟡 Moderate       │
         │  Built into K8s  │ ✅ kubectl apply -k    │ ❌ Needs install   │
         │  GitOps Friendly  │ ✅ Excellent            │ ✅ Good           │
         │  Best For        │ Multi-env, simple apps │ Complex apps,    │
         │                  │                        │ sharing configs   │
         │  Sharing         │ Copy base directory    │ Publish chart repo│
         │  Community       │ CNCF projects default  │ 1000s of charts   │
         │                                                              │
         │  💡 RECOMMENDATION for easypj:                                │
         │     Use Kustomize for easypj app (simple overlays)           │
         │     Use Helm for PostgreSQL/Redis (community charts exist)   │
         │     ArgoCD supports BOTH natively!                           │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    3.6  Namespaces and Multi-Tenancy
         - Using namespaces to separate environments
         - easypj-dev, easypj-staging, easypj-prod
         - How ArgoCD manages multi-namespace deployments
         [DIAGRAM: Namespace layout for easypj environments]

         [EASYPJ NAMESPACE LAYOUT]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  KUBERNETES CLUSTER                                           │
         │  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐ │
         │  │  argocd             ← Where ArgoCD lives                 │ │
         │  │  ┌──────────────┐                                       │ │
         │  │  │ ArgoCD Server │  Manages ALL below                   │ │
         │  │  └──────────────┘                                       │ │
         │  └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘ │
         │                                                              │
         │  ┌─────────────────────┐  ┌─────────────────────┐           │
         │  │  easypj-dev         │  │  easypj-staging      │           │
         │  │  ┌────────────────┐ │  │  ┌────────────────┐  │           │
         │  │  │ easypj:1 rep   │ │  │  │ easypj:2 reps  │  │           │
         │  │  │ postgres:1 rep │ │  │  │ postgres:1 rep │  │           │
         │  │  └────────────────┘ │  │  └────────────────┘  │           │
         │  │  🟢 Dev only        │  │  🟡 Pre-prod testing │           │
         │  └─────────────────────┘  └─────────────────────┘           │
         │                                                              │
         │  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────┐                │
         │  │  easypj-production                       │                │
         │  │  ┌────────────────────────────────────┐ │                │
         │  │  │ easypj:5 replicas (HA)             │ │                │
         │  │  │ postgres:3 replicas (StatefulSet)  │ │                │
         │  │  │ redis:2 replicas                   │ │                │
         │  │  │ ingress: easypj.com                │ │                │
         │  │  └────────────────────────────────────┘ │                │
         │  │  🔴 LIVE TRAFFIC — restricted access     │                │
         │  └─────────────────────────────────────────┘                │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    🔧 UNDER THE HOOD — Why kubectl apply -k Is Just Kustomize + kubectl apply
    ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    Kustomize isn't a separate program bolted onto Kubernetes — since
    kubectl 1.14 it's compiled directly into the kubectl binary. Running
    kubectl apply -k overlays/production does exactly two things: (1)
    kustomize build overlays/production renders the final YAML by merging
    base + patches in memory, then (2) a normal kubectl apply -f - runs
    against the result. ArgoCD's Repo Server does the identical two-step
    internally before ever touching the cluster — which is why anything you
    can kubectl apply -k locally, ArgoCD can sync, with zero surprises.

    ⚠️ GOTCHAS
    ──────────
    • Assuming Kustomize overlays "inherit and modify" YAML like a class
      hierarchy — they don't merge intelligently by default; a patch that
      targets the wrong resource name silently does nothing instead of
      erroring.
    • Storing a Secret's real value in Git "because it's just for dev" —
      Chapter 13 covers why even dev secrets need Sealed Secrets/SOPS;
      habits formed now carry into production.
    • Confusing a Namespace boundary with a security boundary — namespaces
      organize resources, they don't isolate network traffic without a
      NetworkPolicy layered on top.

    ⚡ SPEED TIPS
    ─────────────
    • "Declarative = WHAT, Imperative = HOW" — if a CGOA question's
      kubectl command changes cluster state without a matching Git commit,
      it's imperative and it's wrong for GitOps.
    • Kustomize needs zero extra tooling (built into kubectl); Helm needs
      the helm CLI installed separately — a fast way to eliminate wrong
      answers on "which requires no additional install" questions.
    • Namespace-per-environment (easypj-dev / easypj-staging /
      easypj-production) is the pattern CGOA expects by default unless the
      question says otherwise.

    🔑 COMMAND REFERENCE
    ────────────────────
    ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │ kubectl apply -k overlays/production                                 │
    │     Render + apply a Kustomize overlay in one step.                  │
    │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
    │ kubectl get all -n easypj-prod                                       │
    │     See every resource GitOps has deployed to prod.                  │
    │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
    │ helm install easypj ./easypj-chart                                   │
    │     Install a Helm-packaged dependency (e.g. PostgreSQL).            │
    └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    🏋️ PRACTICE DRILL — Spot the Imperative Command
    ─────────────────────────────────────────────
    Your teammate runs: kubectl set image deployment/easypj-web
    easypj=registry/easypj:v2.2.0 to hotfix a bug, planning to "update Git
    later."
    TASK:
       Rewrite this as the correct GitOps sequence of 3 steps (which file
       changes, what command records it, what happens automatically) — and
       explain what happens to the running v2.2.0 pod if "later" never
       comes.

    📊 VISUAL SUMMARY
    ────────────────
    ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │ ▸ 6 core objects                                                     │
    │   Deployment, Service, ConfigMap, Secret, Ingress, Namespace —       │
    │   each maps to one YAML file in the config repo.                     │
    │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
    │ ▸ Declarative > Imperative                                           │
    │   kubectl commands not backed by a Git commit get overwritten        │
    │   on the next sync — always edit YAML first.                         │
    │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
    │ ▸ Kustomize vs Helm                                                  │
    │   Kustomize (built-in, patch-based) for easypj's own overlays;       │
    │   Helm (templated, external) for community charts like               │
    │   PostgreSQL.                                                        │
    │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
    │ ▸ Namespace-per-env                                                  │
    │   easypj-dev / easypj-staging / easypj-production — isolates         │
    │   environments, scoped RBAC, one cluster.                            │
    └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Chapter 4: GitOps Principles Deep Dive
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    🟡 Intermediate · ⏱️ ~2 hours · 🎯 CGOA: GitOps Fundamentals (30%) · 🏢 Daily-Work: Core mental model for every incident

    WHY THIS CHAPTER MATTERS
    ────────────────────────
    This chapter is the exam's highest-yield single chapter — all 4
    principles, tested from every angle, live here. It's also where Chapter
    1's overview turns into operational muscle memory: when something's
    OutOfSync at 2 AM, you'll reason from these 4 principles, not from
    memory of a diagram.

    🎯 LEARNING OBJECTIVES — by the end of this chapter you can:
    ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
      1. Rewrite any imperative kubectl command as its declarative YAML
         equivalent
      2. Read a git log and explain what each entry changed and why
      3. Trace a drift scenario through Observe → Diff → Act
      4. Place a described GitOps setup at the correct Maturity Model
         level (1-4)
      5. Identify which principle a broken scenario violates

    📍 YOU ARE HERE
    ────────────────
    Part 2: GitOps in Practice ▸ Chapter 1 of 3 ▸ Next: Chapter 5 (Setting Up Your GitOps Environment)

    4.1  Principle 1: Declarative Configuration
         - What "declarative" really means
         - Examples: declarative vs imperative for easypj
         - Why imperative commands break GitOps
         [EXAMPLES: Imperative vs declarative for common tasks]

         [DECLARATIVE IN PRACTICE — easypj operations]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │                                                              │
         │  ❌ IMPERATIVE: "DO this, then that, then this..."            │
         │  $ kubectl scale deployment easypj --replicas=5              │
         │  $ kubectl set image deployment/easypj easypj=v2.1.0        │
         │  $ kubectl annotate deployment easypj "reason=scale"        │
         │  ⚠️ Git has NO idea any of this happened!                    │
         │                                                              │
         │  ✅ DECLARATIVE: "HERE is what I want"                        │
         │  # deployment.yaml (in Git):                                 │
         │  spec:                                                       │
         │    replicas: 5           ← I want 5                          │
         │    template:                                                 │
         │      spec:                                                   │
         │        containers:                                           │
         │        - name: easypj                                       │
         │          image: v2.1.0   ← I want v2.1.0                    │
         │  ✅ Git = Source of Truth. Agent makes it happen.            │
         │                                                              │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    4.2  Principle 2: Versioned and Immutable
         - Every config change = a Git commit
         - Immutability: never edit running configs
         - Audit trail: who changed what, when, why?
         [DIAGRAM: Git history as complete infrastructure audit log]

         [GIT AS INFRASTRUCTURE AUDIT LOG]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │                                                              │
         │  $ git log --oneline -- deployment.yaml                      │
         │                                                              │
         │  abc1234 (HEAD) Rollback: revert to v2.0.1 (bug in v2.1)    │
         │  def5678 Scale easypj to 5 replicas for Black Friday        │
         │  ghi9012 Update easypj image to v2.1.0                       │
         │  jkl3456 Add liveness probe to easypj deployment            │
         │  mno7890 Initial deployment of easypj v1.0.0                 │
         │                                                              │
         │  💡 Every deployment, scale, configuration change —          │
         │     FOREVER recorded with WHO, WHAT, WHEN, and WHY           │
         │                                                              │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    4.3  Principle 3: Pulled Automatically
         - The agent lives INSIDE the cluster
         - Cluster credentials never leave the cluster
         - Why pull is more secure than push
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │ PUSH MODEL (traditional CI/CD)      PULL MODEL (GitOps)                      │
         │ ┌─────────────────────────┐         ┌─────────────────────────┐              │
         │ │ CI runner (external,     │         │ ArgoCD/Flux (INSIDE the │             │
         │ │ outside the cluster)     │         │ cluster, own ServiceAcct)│            │
         │ │                          │         │                          │            │
         │ │ Holds: a kubeconfig or   │         │ Holds: nothing external  │            │
         │ │ cluster-admin token      │         │ — its ServiceAccount     │            │
         │ │                          │         │ token never leaves the   │            │
         │ │ Risk: if CI is           │         │ cluster                  │            │
         │ │ compromised, attacker    │         │                          │            │
         │ │ gets your cluster creds  │         │ Risk: compromising CI    │            │
         │ └───────────┬──────────────┘         │ gets you nothing —       │            │
         │             │ pushes changes IN       │ there are no cluster     │           │
         │             ▼                         │ creds to steal from it   │           │
         │       KUBERNETES CLUSTER              └───────────┬──────────────┘           │
         │                                                    │ pulls FROM Git          │
         │                                                    ▼                         │
         │                                              KUBERNETES CLUSTER              │
         │                                                                              │
         │ 💡 The security boundary moves: in push, the attack surface is                │
         │    "anything that can reach the cluster's API." In pull, it's                │
         │    narrowed to "anything that can write to the Git repo" — and               │
         │    THAT surface already has PR review, signed commits, and                   │
         │    branch protection defending it (Ch2, Ch6, Ch24).                          │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    4.4  Principle 4: Continuously Reconciled
         - The reconciliation loop: observe → compare → act
         - What is "drift"? How GitOps detects it
         - Self-healing: auto-correcting drift
         - Drift scenarios for easypj (someone manually scales pods)
         [DIAGRAM: Reconciliation loop visualization]

         [DRIFT DETECTION — the self-healing magic]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │                                                              │
         │  SCENARIO: Alice manually scales easypj from 3 → 1 pod      │
         │                                                              │
         │  ⏰ T=0: Git says 3, Cluster has 3 → SYNCED ✅               │
         │                                                              │
         │  ⏰ T=1: Alice runs: kubectl scale deploy easypj --repl=1    │
         │         Git says 3, Cluster NOW has 1 → DRIFT DETECTED ⚠️    │
         │                                                              │
         │  ⏰ T=3: ArgoCD reconciliation loop runs:                     │
         │         ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────┐     │
         │         │  1. Read Git: replicas=3                     │     │
         │         │  2. Read Cluster: replicas=1                 │     │
         │         │  3. DIFF: Git ≠ Cluster!                     │     │
         │         │  4. ACTION: kubectl scale → replicas=3       │     │
         │         │  5. Cluster restored to 3 pods               │     │
         │         └─────────────────────────────────────────────┘     │
         │                                                              │
         │  ⏰ T=3: Git says 3, Cluster has 3 → SYNCED ✅ (auto-healed) │
         │                                                              │
         │  💡 Alice's manual change survived for only ~3 minutes!      │
         │  💡 The cluster HEALED ITSELF back to Git's desired state.   │
         │                                                              │
         │  [SYNC STATUS TIMELINE]                                      │
         │  Synced ████████░░████████░░███████████████████ Synced       │
         │          T=0    T=1   T=2    T=3                  time→      │
         │                 ↑Alice         ↑ArgoCD                       │
         │                 scales         heals                         │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    4.5  GitOps Maturity Model
         - Level 1: Git as deployment trigger
         - Level 2: Git as source of truth
         - Level 3: Full reconciliation with auto-healing
         - Level 4: Progressive delivery (canary, blue-green in GitOps)
         [TABLE: GitOps maturity levels with characteristics]

         [GITOPS MATURITY MODEL — detailed]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │                                                              │
         │  LEVEL 1: GIT TRIGGER (Basic)                                │
         │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐   │
         │  │ CI pipeline detects git push → runs kubectl apply    │   │
         │  │ ⚠️ Still PUSH-based, credentials in CI               │   │
         │  │ ⚠️ No drift detection, no auto-healing               │   │
         │  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘   │
         │                                                              │
         │  LEVEL 2: GIT AS SOURCE OF TRUTH (Standard GitOps)           │
         │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐   │
         │  │ GitOps agent pulls from Git, applies to cluster      │   │
         │  │ ✅ Pull-based, credentials in cluster                 │   │
         │  │ ✅ Manual sync or periodic auto-sync                  │   │
         │  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘   │
         │                                                              │
         │  LEVEL 3: FULL RECONCILIATION (Advanced GitOps)              │
         │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐   │
         │  │ + Auto-sync with prune and self-heal                 │   │
         │  │ + Drift detection and automatic correction            │   │
         │  │ + Multi-cluster, App of Apps pattern                  │   │
         │  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘   │
         │                                                              │
         │  LEVEL 4: PROGRESSIVE DELIVERY (Expert GitOps)               │
         │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐   │
         │  │ + Canary deployments with traffic management          │   │
         │  │ + Automated rollback on metric degradation           │   │
         │  │ + Blue-green with automated promotion                │   │
         │  │ + Integration with Service Mesh (Istio, Linkerd)      │   │
         │  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘   │
         │                                                              │
         │  🎯 CGOA TARGET: Level 3 (Full Reconciliation)               │
         │                                                              │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    🔧 UNDER THE HOOD — Why the Sync Interval Is 3 Minutes (and What Happens Between Syncs)
    ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    ArgoCD's default 3-minute polling interval isn't arbitrary — it balances
    API server load (checking every managed resource against Git across
    potentially hundreds of Applications) against how long drift is
    tolerated. Between syncs ArgoCD isn't blind: it also runs a background
    watch on the K8s API for resources it manages, so cluster-side changes
    are often noticed faster than 3 minutes — the loop just doesn't re-run
    the full Git comparison that often. That's exactly why Chapter 11's
    webhook receivers exist: they collapse the wait from "up to 3 minutes"
    to "seconds" by triggering an immediate sync on git push instead of
    waiting for the timer.

    ⚠️ GOTCHAS
    ──────────
    • Believing Level 4 (Progressive Delivery) is "better" than Level 3 in
      every case — CGOA targets Level 3 for most scenarios;
      canary/blue-green add real operational complexity that's only worth
      it for high-traffic services (Part 6 covers when).
    • Assuming reconciliation only fires on a timer — a webhook-triggered
      sync (Ch11) or a manual argocd app sync both trigger the exact same
      Observe→Diff→Act loop immediately, out of band from the 3-minute
      interval.
    • Confusing "Versioned & Immutable" with "you can never change
      anything" — it means every change is a NEW recorded commit, not that
      config is frozen.

    ⚡ SPEED TIPS
    ─────────────
    • Given a scenario, ask "if I typed kubectl right now, would this
      work?" If yes and the exam calls it correct GitOps, the answer is
      wrong — GitOps answers always route through Git first.
    • Maturity Model shortcut: no prune/selfHeal = Level 2. prune+selfHeal
      = Level 3. Canary/blue-green mentioned = Level 4.
    • Drift-timeline questions almost always test whether you know
      self-heal is automatic — no human clicks anything between detection
      and correction.

    🔑 COMMAND REFERENCE
    ────────────────────
    ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │ argocd app diff <app>                                                            │
    │     Preview drift before syncing.                                                │
    │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
    │ argocd app set <app> --sync-policy automated --self-heal                         │
    │     Enable Level 3 auto-sync + self-heal.                                        │
    └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    🏋️ PRACTICE DRILL — Classify the Maturity Level
    ─────────────────────────────────────────────
    Team A's pipeline runs kubectl apply -f whenever CI finishes, with
    deploy credentials stored as a CI secret. Team B has ArgoCD watching
    their config repo with automated: {prune: true, selfHeal: true}.
    TASK:
       Assign each team a Maturity Model level (1-4) and name the ONE
       change Team A would need to make to reach Team B's level.

    📊 VISUAL SUMMARY
    ────────────────
    ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │ ▸ 4 Principles, memorized                                            │
    │   Declarative, Versioned & Immutable, Pulled Automatically,          │
    │   Continuously Reconciled — D-V-P-R.                                 │
    │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
    │ ▸ Reconciliation loop                                                │
    │   Observe cluster → Diff against Git → Act to correct. Repeats       │
    │   every sync interval (~3 min default) or on webhook.                │
    │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
    │ ▸ Drift = self-correcting                                            │
    │   selfHeal:true means manual changes survive only until the          │
    │   next reconciliation pass.                                          │
    │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
    │ ▸ Maturity Model                                                     │
    │   Level 1 Git Trigger → 2 Source of Truth → 3 Full                   │
    │   Reconciliation (CGOA target) → 4 Progressive Delivery.             │
    └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    Chapter 4 — CGOA Exam Focus:
         🎯 All 4 GitOps principles in detail
         🎯 Drift detection and reconciliation
         🎯 GitOps maturity model

    5.4  Installing FluxCD

         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐

         │     --owner=yourorg \                                                        │
         │     --repository=easypj-config \                                             │

         │     --path=clusters/dev \                                                    │
         │     --personal                                                               │
         │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
         │ EXPECTED OUTPUT:                                                             │

         │   ► cloning branch 'main' from Git repository                                │
         │   ► generating component manifests                                           │
         │   ► component manifests are up to date                                       │

         │   ► Source Controller: deployment ready                                      │
         │   ✔ bootstrap finished                                                       │
         │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
         │ This one command: installs all 5 Flux controllers, creates the               │
         │ flux-system namespace, generates a GitRepository pointing back at            │
         │ easypj-config itself, and commits that bootstrap config into                 │
         │ clusters/dev/flux-system/ — Flux is now managing its OWN manifests.          │

         │  │  │  📋 ConfigMap/easypj-cfg  1 config              │  │   │
         │  │  │  🔒 Secret/easypj-db      1 secret (sealed)     │  │   │
         │  │  │  🌍 Ingress/easypj       easypj.com            │  │   │
         │  │  └────────────────────────────────────────────────┘  │   │
         │  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘   │
         │                                                              │
         │  VERIFY:                                                     │
         │  $ kubectl get all -n easypj-prod                            │
         │  NAME                          READY   STATUS    RESTARTS    │
         │  pod/easypj-5d8f7b9c4-abcde    1/1     Running   0          │
         │  pod/easypj-5d8f7b9c4-fghij    1/1     Running   0          │
         │  pod/easypj-5d8f7b9c4-klmno    1/1     Running   0          │
         │                                                              │
         │  ✅ easypj is LIVE via GitOps! 🎉                            │
         │                                                              │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    🔧 UNDER THE HOOD — What kubectl apply -f install.yaml Actually Installs
    ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    That one install.yaml is a bundle of roughly a dozen K8s resources:
    Deployments for each ArgoCD component, Services to expose them
    internally, ConfigMaps holding argocd-cm and argocd-rbac-cm (the two
    ConfigMaps you'll edit constantly from Chapter 9 onward), Secrets for
    the initial admin password and TLS certs, and the CRDs that define
    Application and AppProject themselves. This matters practically: when
    ArgoCD "won't start," kubectl get pods -n argocd and checking which of
    these dozen resources isn't healthy is always the first troubleshooting
    step, before touching the argocd CLI at all.

    ⚠️ GOTCHAS
    ──────────
    • Forgetting to port-forward (or set up Ingress) and assuming ArgoCD
      "isn't working" when the UI simply isn't reachable yet — kubectl
      port-forward only lasts for the life of that terminal session.
    • Never rotating the initial admin password — Chapter 24 covers why
      the default admin account should be disabled entirely in favor of
      SSO once you're past this learning setup.
    • Creating the Application with the wrong --dest-namespace and
      wondering why kubectl get pods in a different namespace shows
      nothing.

    ⚡ SPEED TIPS
    ─────────────
    • k3d is the fastest local cluster (~30 sec) if a question is about
      setup speed specifically; Kind is the more common CI-testing answer.
    • argocd app diff before argocd app sync is a habit CGOA rewards — it
      appears as the "best practice" answer in multiple scenario
      questions.
    • --auto-prune without --self-heal only gets you to the Maturity Level
      2/3 boundary — both flags together are what Chapter 4's Level 3
      actually requires.

    🔑 COMMAND REFERENCE
    ────────────────────
    ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │ kubectl apply -n argocd -f install.yaml                                          │
    │     Install ArgoCD's core components.                                            │
    │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
    │ argocd app create <app> --repo <url> --sync-policy automated --auto-prune        │
    │     Create an auto-syncing Application.                                          │
    │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
    │ kubectl get all -n easypj-prod                                                   │
    │     Verify the deployment from the cluster side.                                 │
    └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Chapter 6: Git Repositories for GitOps
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    🟢 Beginner · ⏱️ ~1 hour · 🎯 CGOA: GitOps Fundamentals (30%) · 🏢 Daily-Work: Repo structure decisions

    WHY THIS CHAPTER MATTERS
    ────────────────────────
    The repo-structure decisions in this chapter are made ONCE, early, and
    are expensive to reverse later — Chapter 27's enterprise-scale patterns
    all assume you got this foundation right.

    🎯 LEARNING OBJECTIVES — by the end of this chapter you can:
    ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
      1. Compare mono-repo, separate-repo, and per-environment repo
         patterns
      2. Explain why environment branches are a GitOps anti-pattern
      3. Configure branch protection and CODEOWNERS for a config repo
      4. Choose directory-based over branch-based environments, and
         justify it
      5. Recommend mono- vs multi-repo for a team of a given size

    📍 YOU ARE HERE
    ────────────────
    Part 2: GitOps in Practice ▸ Chapter 3 of 3 ▸ Next: Part 3 — ArgoCD Powerhouse (Chapter 7)

    6.1  Repository Structure Patterns
         - Single repo (monorepo) pattern
         - Separate app + config repos (recommended)
         - Per-environment repos
         - Choosing the right pattern for your team
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │ MONOREPO              SEPARATE APP+CONFIG    PER-ENVIRONMENT                 │
         │ (recommended: NO)      (recommended: YES)     (recommended: NO)              │
         │ ┌─────────────────┐    ┌────────┐┌────────┐   ┌────────┐┌────────┐           │
         │ │ easypj/          │    │ easypj  ││ easypj- │   │ config- ││ config- │      │
         │ │  app code +      │    │ (code)  ││ config  │   │ dev     ││ prod    │      │
         │ │  K8s YAML mixed  │    │         ││ (YAML)  │   │         ││         │      │
         │ └─────────────────┘    └────────┘└────────┘   └────────┘└────────┘           │
         │ Devs and ops share      Devs own app repo,     Requires manual               │
         │ one repo, one PR         ops own config repo    promotion between            │
         │ queue — ownership         — clean access         repos, easy to              │
         │ gets blurry fast          control split           drift out of sync          │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    6.2  Branching Strategies for GitOps
         - Trunk-Based Development (recommended)
         - Environment branches (dev/staging/prod)
         - Feature branches with PR gates
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │ TRUNK-BASED (recommended)          ENVIRONMENT BRANCHES (anti-pattern)       │
         │ main ──●──●──●──●──●──▶            main ──●───────────────▶                  │
         │         │  │     │                        │                                  │
         │         ▼  ▼     ▼                        ├─▶ dev    ──●──●──▶               │
         │      short-lived feature                   ├─▶ staging ──●────▶              │
         │      branches, merged in                   └─▶ prod   ──●──▶                 │
         │      hours/days, deleted                                                     │
         │                                     Promoting a fix means manually           │
         │      Git history = deploy           cherry-picking/merging across 3          │
         │      history, always current        branches — easy to miss one and          │
         │                                     silently drift.                          │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    6.3  Git Repository Security
         - Branch protection rules
         - Required reviewers for config changes
         - Signed commits and verification
         - CODEOWNERS file for config repo
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │ # GitHub branch protection for easypj-config's main branch                   │
         │ # (via UI: Settings > Branches > Add rule, or via API/Terraform):            │
         │                                                                              │
         │   Branch name pattern:        main                                           │
         │   Require a pull request:     YES (1+ approvals)                             │
         │   Require review from                                                        │
         │     Code Owners:              YES                                            │
         │   Require signed commits:     YES                                            │
         │   Require status checks:      YES (secret-scan, yamllint)                    │
         │   Require branches to be                                                     │
         │     up to date before merge:  YES                                            │
         │   Restrict who can push:      platform-team only (no direct pushes)          │
         │   Allow force pushes:         NO                                             │
         │   Allow deletions:            NO                                             │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    6.4  Multiple Environments with GitOps
         - How to handle dev, staging, production
         - Directory-based environments (base/overlays)
         - Branch-based environments
         - Kustomize overlays for easypj environments
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │ easypj-config/                                                               │
         │   base/                                                                      │
         │     deployment.yaml       # replicas, image — no env-specific values         │
         │     service.yaml                                                             │
         │     kustomization.yaml                                                       │
         │   overlays/                                                                  │
         │     dev/                                                                     │
         │       kustomization.yaml  # patches: replicas=1, TAG=dev-latest              │
         │     staging/                                                                 │
         │       kustomization.yaml  # patches: replicas=2, TAG=staging                 │
         │     prod/                                                                    │
         │       kustomization.yaml  # patches: replicas=5, TAG=v2.4.1                  │
         │                                                                              │
         │ ONE branch (main), THREE overlays — an ArgoCD ApplicationSet's               │
         │ Git directory generator (Ch10.2) turns each overlays/<env>/ into             │
         │ its own Application automatically, no manual per-env setup.                  │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    6.5  Mono-Repo vs Multi-Repo for easypj
         - Pros and cons of each approach
         - When mono-repo makes sense
         - When multi-repo is better
         - Recommended structure for easypj
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │ START: "Should easypj use mono-repo or multi-repo config?"                   │
         │   │                                                                          │
         │   ├── Is it ONE team, ONE app, low complexity?                               │
         │   │     ├── YES ─▶ Mono-repo is fine — less overhead to manage               │
         │   │     └── NO  ─▶ continue                                                  │
         │   │                                                                          │
         │   ├── Do different teams need different access/review rules?                 │
         │   │     ├── YES ─▶ Multi-repo (repo-per-team) — Ch27.1                       │
         │   │     └── NO  ─▶ continue                                                  │
         │   │                                                                          │
         │   ├── Will this scale past ~10 applications?                                 │
         │   │     ├── YES ─▶ Multi-repo, or a monorepo with strict                     │
         │   │     │          teams/ directories (Ch27's structure)                     │
         │   │     └── NO  ─▶ continue                                                  │
         │   │                                                                          │
         │   └── Default for easypj at its current size: separate app +                 │
         │       config repos (6.1), single config repo, directory-based                │
         │       overlays (6.4) — revisit only if the team count grows.                 │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    🔧 UNDER THE HOOD — Why Environment Branches Actually Cause Drift
    ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    It's tempting to model dev/staging/production as three long-lived
    branches, since that maps neatly to how the environments feel
    conceptually separate. But ArgoCD/Flux each watch ONE targetRevision
    (branch/tag) per Application — so three environment branches means three
    branches that must be kept in sync by hand via cherry-picks or merges,
    which is exactly the manual-drift-prone process GitOps exists to
    eliminate. Directory-based environments (base/ + overlays/dev, staging,
    production) solve this because all three environments live on the SAME
    branch (main) at all times — there's only ever one source of truth to
    diff against, and promoting a change from staging to production is a PR
    that changes which directory an Application points to, not a
    cross-branch merge.

    ⚠️ GOTCHAS
    ──────────
    • Choosing per-environment repos "for isolation" without realizing it
      triples the CODEOWNERS/branch-protection maintenance burden —
      Section 6.5's decision tree exists because this trade-off is easy to
      underestimate.
    • Signed commits and CODEOWNERS enforce WHO can approve; they do
      nothing to stop a bad YAML value from an authorized person — pair
      them with the pre-commit validation from Chapter 2.
    • Assuming mono-repo (app + config together) is always simpler — it
      removes the access-control separation from Chapter 1's two-repo
      pattern, which matters the moment a team grows past "everyone trusts
      everyone."

    ⚡ SPEED TIPS
    ─────────────
    • "Environment = directory, not branch" is a one-line answer to most
      of this chapter's exam questions.
    • If the question describes cherry-picking between environment
      branches as a workflow, that's the anti-pattern — the correct answer
      is always the overlay pattern.
    • Small team (2-5 devs), one product → separate app+config repos is
      the default recommendation. Only reach for per-environment repos at
      real enterprise scale (Chapter 27).

    🔑 COMMAND REFERENCE
    ────────────────────
    None yet — this chapter is conceptual. Your first real
    commands appear in the next hands-on chapter.

    🏋️ PRACTICE DRILL — Pick the Right Repo Pattern
    ─────────────────────────────────────────────
    A 4-person team is building easypj from scratch. They've never used
    GitOps before and want the simplest correct setup.
    TASK:
       Recommend one of the 3 repo patterns from 6.1, and specify: how
       many repos, what goes in each, and whether they need environment
       branches or directories.

    📊 VISUAL SUMMARY
    ────────────────
    ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │ ▸ 3 repo patterns                                                    │
    │   Mono-repo (simplest), separate app+config (recommended             │
    │   default), per-environment (enterprise scale only).                 │
    │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
    │ ▸ Environment = directory                                            │
    │   base/ + overlays/{dev,staging,production} on ONE branch —          │
    │   never separate long-lived environment branches.                    │
    │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
    │ ▸ Repo security                                                      │
    │   Branch protection + required reviews + CODEOWNERS + signed         │
    │   commits — layered defense for the config repo specifically.        │
    │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
    │ ▸ Default for small teams                                            │
    │   2 repos (app + config), directory-based environments,              │
    │   Trunk-Based Development — the pattern used throughout this         │
    │   guide.                                                             │
    └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    Chapter 6 — CGOA Exam Focus:
         🎯 Repository structure patterns
         🎯 Branching strategies for GitOps
         🎯 Multi-environment management

Chapter 7: ArgoCD Architecture Deep Dive
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    🟡 Intermediate · ⏱️ ~2 hours · 🎯 CGOA: ArgoCD (30%) · 🏢 Daily-Work: Diagnosing "why won't it sync"

    WHY THIS CHAPTER MATTERS
    ────────────────────────
    Part 3 is 30% of the exam by itself, and this chapter is its foundation
    — every troubleshooting chapter later (Appendix D) assumes you know
    which of ArgoCD's 5 components is responsible for which failure mode.

    🎯 LEARNING OBJECTIVES — by the end of this chapter you can:

         syncPolicy

         scenario

    7.1  ArgoCD Components
         - API Server (REST API + gRPC)
         - Repository Server (clones Git repos)
         - Application Controller (reconciliation engine)
         - Redis Cache (performance)
         - Dex (SSO/OIDC authentication)
         - Notification Controller
         - ApplicationSet Controller
         [DIAGRAM: ArgoCD component architecture]

         [ARGOCD ARCHITECTURE — detailed component view]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │                    ARGOCD NAMESPACE                           │
         │                                                              │
         │  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐ │
         │  │                     👤 USERS                              │ │
         │  │  ┌──────────┐  ┌──────────────┐  ┌──────────────────┐  │ │
         │  │  │  Browser │  │  argocd CLI  │  │  kubectl apply   │  │ │
         │  │  │  (UI)    │  │  (terminal)  │  │  (Application)   │  │ │
         │  │  └────┬─────┘  └──────┬───────┘  └────────┬─────────┘  │ │
         │  │       │               │                    │            │ │
         │  │       └───────────────┼────────────────────┘            │ │
         │  │                       │ gRPC/REST                        │ │
         │  │                       ▼                                  │ │
         │  │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────┐  │ │
         │  │  │  🌐 API SERVER (argocd-server)                    │  │ │
         │  │  │  • Serves Web UI and CLI                          │  │ │
         │  │  │  • Authentication (JWT, OIDC via Dex)             │  │ │
         │  │  │  • RBAC enforcement                               │  │ │
         │  │  │  • Audit logging                                  │  │ │
         │  │  └──────────────────────┬───────────────────────────┘  │ │
         │  │                         │                                │ │
         │  │  ┌──────────────────────┼───────────────────────────┐  │ │
         │  │  │                      ▼                             │  │ │
         │  │  │  ⚙️ APPLICATION CONTROLLER                         │  │ │
         │  │  │  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────┐ │  │ │
         │  │  │  │  1. Reads Application CRDs                   │ │  │ │
         │  │  │  │  2. Calls Repo Server to clone Git           │ │  │ │
         │  │  │  │  3. Renders manifests (Kustomize/Helm/plain) │ │  │ │
         │  │  │  │  4. Compares Git vs Cluster (diff)            │ │  │ │
         │  │  │  │  5. Syncs if different (kubectl apply)       │ │  │ │
         │  │  │  │  6. Updates status back to Application       │ │  │ │
         │  │  │  └─────────────────────────────────────────────┘ │  │ │
         │  │  └──────────────────────┬───────────────────────────┘  │ │
         │  │                         │                                │ │
         │  │  ┌──────────────────────┼───────────────────────────┐  │ │
         │  │  │  📦 REPOSITORY SERVER  │  📊 REDIS CACHE          │  │ │
         │  │  │  Clones Git repos into │  Caches Git repos +      │  │ │
         │  │  │  local storage for     │  cluster state for fast   │  │ │
         │  │  │  manifest generation   │  comparison               │  │ │
         │  │  └──────────────────────┘  └──────────────────────────┘  │ │
         │  │                                                           │ │
         │  │  ┌──────────────────────┐  ┌──────────────────────────┐  │ │
         │  │  │  🔔 NOTIFICATIONS     │  │  📋 APPLICATIONSET CTRL   │  │ │
         │  │  │  Sends alerts on sync │  │  Generates Applications   │  │ │
         │  │  │  status to Slack/     │  │  from templates using      │  │ │
         │  │  │  Email/Webhooks       │  │  generators (Git, List,etc)│  │ │
         │  │  └──────────────────────┘  └──────────────────────────┘  │ │
         │  └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘ │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    7.2  How ArgoCD Works — The Reconciliation Engine
         - Git repository → Repository Server clone
         - Application Controller reads manifests
         - Compares desired (Git) vs actual (K8s)
         - Syncs if there's a difference
         - Health assessment after sync
         [STEP-BY-STEP FLOW: Reconciliation cycle]

         [RECONCILIATION LOOP — step by step]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │                                                              │
         │  EVERY 3 MINUTES (by default):                               │
         │                                                              │
         │  STEP 1: FETCH                                               │
         │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐   │
         │  │  Repo Server: git pull origin main                   │   │
         │  │  "Has the config repo changed?"                       │   │
         │  │  If NO → skip to end (nothing to do)                 │   │
         │  │  If YES → continue to Step 2                         │   │
         │  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘   │
         │           │                                                   │
         │           ▼                                                   │
         │  STEP 2: RENDER                                               │
         │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐   │
         │  │  Read deployment.yaml (or run Kustomize/Helm)        │   │
         │  │  Generate final manifests:                           │   │
         │  │    apiVersion: apps/v1                               │   │
         │  │    kind: Deployment                                  │   │
         │  │    spec:                                             │   │
         │  │      replicas: 3  ← THIS IS WHAT GIT WANTS          │   │
         │  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘   │
         │           │                                                   │
         │           ▼                                                   │
         │  STEP 3: COMPARE                                              │
         │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐   │
         │  │  kubectl get deployment easypj -o yaml                │   │
         │  │    spec:                                             │   │
         │  │      replicas: 2  ← THIS IS WHAT CLUSTER HAS         │   │
         │  │                                                      │   │
         │  │  DIFF: Git=3, Cluster=2 → OUT OF SYNC!               │   │
         │  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘   │
         │           │                                                   │
         │           ▼                                                   │
         │  STEP 4: SYNC (if auto-sync enabled)                         │
         │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐   │
         │  │  kubectl apply -f deployment.yaml                    │   │
         │  │  → Cluster now has 3 replicas                        │   │
         │  │  → Status: SYNCED ✅                                  │   │
         │  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘   │
         │           │                                                   │
         │           ▼                                                   │
         │  STEP 5: HEALTH CHECK                                         │
         │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐   │
         │  │  Are pods running? Are they healthy?                  │   │
         │  │  → 3/3 pods running → HEALTHY ✅                      │   │
         │  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘   │
         │                                                              │
         │  Result: App shows: "Synced" + "Healthy" 🟢                  │
         │                                                              │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    7.3  The Application CRD
         - What is an ArgoCD Application?
         - Key fields: source, destination, syncPolicy
         - Application vs App of Apps pattern
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │ apiVersion: argoproj.io/v1alpha1                                             │
         │ kind: Application                                                            │
         │ metadata:                                                                    │
         │   name: easypj-web                                                           │
         │   namespace: argocd                                                          │
         │   finalizers:                                                                │
         │     - resources-finalizer.argocd.argoproj.io                                 │
         │ spec:                                                                        │
         │   project: easypj                                                            │
         │   source:                                                                    │
         │     repoURL: https://github.com/yourorg/easypj-config.git                    │
         │     targetRevision: main                                                     │
         │     path: overlays/prod                                                      │
         │   destination:                                                               │
         │     server: https://kubernetes.default.svc                                   │
         │     namespace: easypj                                                        │
         │   syncPolicy:                                                                │
         │     automated:                                                               │
         │       prune: true                                                            │
         │       selfHeal: true                                                         │
         │     syncOptions:                                                             │
         │       - CreateNamespace=true                                                 │
         │     retry:                                                                   │
         │       limit: 5                                                               │
         │       backoff: {duration: 5s, factor: 2, maxDuration: 3m}                    │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    7.4  Sync Strategies and Policies
         - Manual sync vs Auto-sync
         - Prune resources (auto-delete removed manifests)
         - Self-heal (auto-fix drift)
         - Sync waves and hooks
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │ prune   │ selfHeal │ RESULTING BEHAVIOR                                      │
         │ ─────────┼──────────┼────────────────────────────────────────                │
         │ false   │ false    │ Manual-only: nothing auto-corrects,                     │
         │         │          │ nothing auto-deletes. Safest, slowest.                  │
         │ ─────────┼──────────┼────────────────────────────────────────                │
         │ true    │ false    │ Resources removed from Git ARE deleted                  │
         │         │          │ automatically; manual field edits on                    │
         │         │          │ resources still in Git are left alone.                  │
         │ ─────────┼──────────┼────────────────────────────────────────                │
         │ false   │ true     │ Manual field edits ARE reverted; removed-               │
         │         │          │ from-Git resources are NOT auto-deleted                 │
         │         │          │ (they just show as OutOfSync forever).                  │
         │ ─────────┼──────────┼────────────────────────────────────────                │
         │ true    │ true     │ Full Reconciliation (Maturity Level 3,                  │
         │         │          │ Ch4.5) — Git is fully authoritative in                  │
         │         │          │ both directions. Production default.                    │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    7.5  ArgoCD Health Checks
         - How ArgoCD determines app health
         - Health status: Healthy, Progressing, Degraded, Suspended, Missing
         - Custom health checks with Lua scripts
         - Health check for easypj Django app
         [DIAGRAM: Health check flow for a Deployment]

    🔧 UNDER THE HOOD — Why Redis Sits in the Critical Path
    ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    Redis isn't a nice-to-have cache you could remove — the Application
    Controller consults it on every reconciliation pass to avoid re-cloning
    and re-diffing every Git repo and every cluster resource from scratch.
    At even moderate scale (dozens of Applications), skipping the cache
    would make each 3-minute cycle take far longer than 3 minutes. This is
    also why "restart argocd-redis" is a real, frequently-cited fix in
    Appendix D for a reconciliation loop that seems stuck — a stale or
    corrupted cache can make ArgoCD believe it already checked something it
    hasn't.

    ⚠️ GOTCHAS
    ──────────
    • Assuming the API Server does the actual syncing — it only serves the
      UI/CLI and enforces auth; the Application Controller is the
      component that reads Applications and applies changes.
    • Setting prune: true without selfHeal: true and expecting drift to
      auto-correct — prune only removes resources deleted from Git, it
      does nothing about a manually-changed field like replica count.
    • Treating "Degraded" and "Missing" as interchangeable — Degraded
      means ArgoCD found the resource and it's unhealthy; Missing means
      the resource doesn't exist in the cluster at all.

    ⚡ SPEED TIPS
    ─────────────
    • 5 components, 5 jobs: API Server=auth/serve, Repo Server=clone Git,
      App Controller=diff+apply, Redis=cache, Dex=SSO. Memorize the
      pairing, not just the list.
    • Health status order to remember: Healthy > Progressing > Suspended >
      Missing > Degraded (roughly best to worst) — a question asking
      "which is worse" almost always means Degraded.
    • syncPolicy.automated: {} with no flags = auto-sync only, no prune,
      no self-heal — the minimal, most conservative auto-sync config.

    🔑 COMMAND REFERENCE
    ────────────────────
    ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │ argocd app get <app>                                                             │
    │     Full status: sync state, health, and resource tree.                          │
    │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
    │ kubectl get pods -n argocd                                                       │
    │     Check which of the 5 ArgoCD components isn't healthy.                        │
    └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    🏋️ PRACTICE DRILL — Trace a Sync From Component to Component
    ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    You just ran git push to easypj-config. 2 minutes later, argocd app get
    easypj-prod shows Synced/Healthy.
    TASK:
       Name, in order, the 3 ArgoCD components that were involved between
       your push and that result, and the one action each performed.

    📊 VISUAL SUMMARY
    ────────────────
    ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │ ▸ 5 components                                                       │
    │   API Server, Repo Server, Application Controller, Redis, Dex —      │
    │   each owns exactly one job.                                         │
    │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
    │ ▸ 5-step loop                                                        │
    │   Fetch → Render → Compare → Sync → Health check, every ~3           │
    │   minutes.                                                           │
    │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
    │ ▸ Sync policy flags                                                  │
    │   prune (delete removed) + selfHeal (auto-fix drift) =               │
    │   production-recommended Level 3 config.                             │
    │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
    │ ▸ 5 health statuses                                                  │
    │   Healthy, Progressing, Degraded, Suspended, Missing.                │
    └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    WHY THIS CHAPTER MATTERS

         multi-source) for a given app

    📍 YOU ARE HERE
