Chapter 16: FluxCD Fundamentals
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    🟡 Intermediate · ⏱️ ~2 hours · 🎯 CGOA: FluxCD (20%) · 🏢 Daily-Work: A different mental model

    WHY THIS CHAPTER MATTERS
    ────────────────────────
    Part 5 is 20% of the exam and, more importantly, teaches an
    architectural philosophy — fault-isolated micro-controllers — that shows
    up again the moment you touch any cloud-native controller-runtime
    project, not just Flux.

    🎯 LEARNING OBJECTIVES — by the end of this chapter you can:
    ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
      1. Name Flux's 5 controllers and the CRD each one owns
      2. Explain why Flux's architecture gives it fault isolation that
         ArgoCD's doesn't have
      3. Run flux bootstrap and explain what it commits to your repo
      4. Write a minimal GitRepository + Kustomization pair for
         easypj-config

    📍 YOU ARE HERE
    ────────────────
    Part 5: FluxCD ▸ Chapter 1 of 5 ▸ Next: Chapter 17 (FluxCD Helm Integration)

    16.1 What is FluxCD?
          - FluxCD origins and CNCF graduation
          - Flux vs ArgoCD: philosophical differences
          - Flux's "micro-controller" architecture
          [DIAGRAM: FluxCD component architecture]

         [FLUXCD ARCHITECTURE — micro-controller model]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  flux-system namespace                                       │
         │  ┌──────────────┐ ┌─────────────┐ ┌──────────────────┐     │
         │  │Source Ctrl   │ │Kustomize    │ │Helm Controller   │     │
         │  │(Git/Helm/OCI)│ │Controller   │ │(HelmRelease CRD) │     │
         │  └──────┬───────┘ └──────┬──────┘ └────────┬─────────┘     │
         │         │                │                   │               │
         │         ▼                ▼                   ▼               │
         │  ┌──────────────┐ ┌─────────────┐ ┌──────────────────┐     │
         │  │Notification  │ │Image Auto   │ │Each controller   │     │
         │  │Controller    │ │Controller   │ │runs independently│     │
         │  │(Slack/Teams) │ │(auto-update)│ │✅ fault isolation│     │
         │  └──────────────┘ └─────────────┘ └──────────────────┘     │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    16.2 FluxCD Components
          - Source Controller (GitRepository, HelmRepository, Bucket)
          - Kustomize Controller (kustomization reconciliation)
          - Helm Controller (HelmRelease management)
          - Notification Controller (alerts)
          - Image Automation Controller (auto-update images)
          [TABLE: Flux controllers and their CRDs]

          📍 The Source Controller's Bucket and OCIRepository kinds are real,
          production State Store alternatives to Git — Chapter 33.3 explains
          why a team would choose one over GitRepository.

    16.3 Flux Installation and Bootstrap
          - flux bootstrap github/gitlab
          - Bootstrapping connects Flux to your Git repo
          - What bootstrap creates in your repo
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │ $ flux bootstrap github --owner=yourorg --repository=easypj-config \         │
         │     --path=clusters/dev --personal                                           │
         │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
         │ WHAT THIS CREATES IN clusters/dev/flux-system/:                              │
         │   gotk-components.yaml   # all 5 controllers' Deployments/CRDs/RBAC          │
         │   gotk-sync.yaml          # a GitRepository + Kustomization pointing         │
         │                           # Flux back at THIS SAME repo/path                 │
         │   kustomization.yaml      # ties the two files above together                │
         │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
         │ This is the detail most guides skip: Flux bootstraps itself INTO             │
         │ GitOps management. After this command, if you delete gotk-sync.yaml          │
         │ from Git, Flux's OWN Kustomization Controller would (on next                 │
         │ reconcile) remove itself — Flux manages Flux, same as everything             │
         │ else it manages.                                                             │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    16.4 Flux Sources — GitRepository
          - GitRepository CRD: where to pull from
          - Branches, tags, semver ranges
          - Authentication: SSH vs HTTPS
          - Interval: how often to check
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │ apiVersion: source.toolkit.fluxcd.io/v1                                      │
         │ kind: GitRepository                                                          │
         │ metadata:                                                                    │
         │   name: easypj-config                                                        │
         │   namespace: flux-system                                                     │
         │ spec:                                                                        │
         │   interval: 1m                                                               │
         │   url: https://github.com/yourorg/easypj-config.git                          │
         │   ref:                                                                       │
         │     branch: main                                                             │
         │     # OR: tag: v2.4.1                                                        │
         │     # OR: semver: '>=2.0.0 <3.0.0'                                           │
         │   secretRef:                                                                 │
         │     name: easypj-config-auth   # SSH key or HTTPS token Secret               │
         │   ignore: |                                                                  │
         │     /docs                                                                    │
         │     /*.md                                                                    │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    16.5 Flux Kustomization — The Reconciliation Engine
          - Kustomization CRD: what to apply
          - Path, prune, health checks
          - DependsOn for ordering
          - Drift detection and correction
          [CODE BLOCK: Kustomization for easypj]

    🔧 UNDER THE HOOD — Why Flux Calls Itself a "Toolkit," Not a Server
    ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    ArgoCD ships as one API Server plus one Application Controller — a
    single blast radius. Flux is architecturally a set of independent
    controllers (Source, Kustomize, Helm, Notification, Image) each built on
    the same generic controller-runtime library every native Kubernetes
    controller uses, communicating only through Kubernetes custom resources
    (a GitRepository object, not a direct function call). If the Image
    Automation controller crashes, the Kustomize controller keeps
    reconciling — they don't share a process, a cache, or a failure. This is
    the literal meaning of "fault isolation," and it's also why Flux has no
    single admin UI: there's no single process left to serve one from.

    ⚠️ GOTCHAS
    ──────────
    • Assuming flux bootstrap only installs software — it also PUSHES the
      initial Flux manifests (gotk-components.yaml, gotk-sync.yaml) into
      your Git repo, meaning Flux ends up managing its own installation
      via GitOps, recursively.
    • Expecting a GitRepository alone to deploy anything — it only
      fetches; you need a Kustomization (or HelmRelease) pointing at that
      source before anything gets applied to the cluster.
    • Comparing Flux's lack of rich UI to "missing a feature" rather than
      a design trade-off — it trades UI convenience for the fault
      isolation and Kubernetes-native simplicity described above.

    ⚡ SPEED TIPS
    ─────────────
    • 5 controllers → 5 CRD families: Source
      (GitRepository/HelmRepository/OCIRepository/Bucket), Kustomize
      (Kustomization), Helm (HelmRelease/HelmChart), Notification
      (Provider/Alert), Image Automation
      (ImageRepository/ImagePolicy/ImageUpdateAutomation).
    • "Fault isolation" and "micro-controller" are the two words CGOA uses
      to signal a Flux-architecture question.
    • flux bootstrap = install + connect + push initial config, all in one
      command — functionally Flux's equivalent of ArgoCD's install.yaml +
      first Application create, combined.

    🔑 COMMAND REFERENCE
    ────────────────────
    ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │ flux bootstrap github --owner=<org> --repository=easypj-config                   │
    │ --path=clusters/production                                                       │
    │     Install Flux, connect it to Git, and push initial config.                    │
    │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
    │ flux get all -A                                                                  │
    │     Check readiness of every Flux-managed resource across namespaces.            │
    └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    🏋️ PRACTICE DRILL — Match Controller to CRD
    ─────────────────────────────────────────
    Your easypj-config repo has a GitRepository resource that's Ready=True,
    but nothing is showing up in the cluster.
    TASK:
       Name the ONE resource type you're missing, which controller owns
       it, and why a GitRepository being Ready doesn't imply anything is
       deployed.

    📊 VISUAL SUMMARY
    ────────────────
    ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │ ▸ 5 controllers                                                      │
    │   Source, Kustomize, Helm, Notification, Image Automation —          │
    │   each independent, each owning its own CRDs.                        │
    │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
    │ ▸ Fault isolation                                                    │
    │   One controller crashing doesn't affect the others — no shared      │
    │   process, unlike ArgoCD's monolith.                                 │
    │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
    │ ▸ flux bootstrap                                                     │
    │   One command: installs controllers, connects to Git, pushes         │
    │   initial config — Flux ends up managing itself.                     │
    │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
    │ ▸ Source ≠ deploy                                                    │
    │   GitRepository only fetches. A Kustomization or HelmRelease is      │
    │   what actually applies anything to the cluster.                     │
    └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    Chapter 16 — CGOA Exam Focus:
         🎯 FluxCD architecture and components
         🎯 Bootstrap process
         🎯 GitRepository and Kustomization CRDs


Chapter 17: FluxCD Helm Integration
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    🟡 Intermediate · ⏱️ ~1.5 hours · 🎯 CGOA: FluxCD (20%) · 🏢 Daily-Work: Deploying easypj's dependencies (PostgreSQL, Redis)

    WHY THIS CHAPTER MATTERS
    ────────────────────────
    easypj doesn't run alone — PostgreSQL and Redis are best deployed from
    community Helm charts, and this chapter is where Flux's HelmRelease
    replaces manual helm install with something GitOps-managed and
    self-healing.

    🎯 LEARNING OBJECTIVES — by the end of this chapter you can:
    ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
      1. Write a HelmRelease that installs a chart with custom values
      2. Pull a value from a Kubernetes Secret using valuesFrom instead of
         hardcoding it
      3. Decide when to wrap a HelmRelease in Kustomize overlays vs when
         not to
      4. Explain what upgrade.remediation.remediateLastFailure does

    📍 YOU ARE HERE
    ────────────────
    Part 5: FluxCD ▸ Chapter 2 of 5 ▸ Next: Chapter 18 (FluxCD Image Automation)

    17.1 HelmRelease CRD
          - Declarative Helm releases via Flux
          - Values, valuesFrom (ConfigMaps/Secrets)
          - Upgrade strategies and rollback
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │ apiVersion: helm.toolkit.fluxcd.io/v2beta2                                   │
         │ kind: HelmRelease                                                            │
         │ metadata:                                                                    │
         │   name: postgres                                                             │
         │   namespace: easypj                                                          │
         │ spec:                                                                        │
         │   interval: 10m                                                              │
         │   chart:                                                                     │
         │     spec:                                                                    │
         │       chart: postgresql                                                      │
         │       version: '13.x'                                                        │
         │       sourceRef:                                                             │
         │         kind: HelmRepository                                                 │
         │         name: bitnami                                                        │
         │         namespace: flux-system                                               │
         │   values:                                                                    │
         │     auth:                                                                    │
         │       database: easypj                                                       │
         │   valuesFrom:                                                                │
         │     - kind: Secret                                                           │
         │       name: postgres-admin-creds                                             │
         │       valuesKey: password                                                    │
         │       targetPath: auth.postgresPassword                                      │
         │   upgrade:                                                                   │
         │     remediation:                                                             │
         │       remediateLastFailure: true   # auto-rollback a failed upgrade          │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    17.2 HelmRepository CRD
          - Connecting to Helm repositories
          - Public repos (Bitnami, ingress-nginx)
          - Private repos with authentication
          - OCI Helm repositories
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │ # Public Helm repo (Bitnami)                                                 │
         │ apiVersion: source.toolkit.fluxcd.io/v1                                      │
         │ kind: HelmRepository                                                         │
         │ metadata:                                                                    │
         │   name: bitnami                                                              │
         │   namespace: flux-system                                                     │
         │ spec:                                                                        │
         │   url: https://charts.bitnami.com/bitnami                                    │
         │   interval: 1h                                                               │
         │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
         │ # Private repo with authentication                                           │
         │ apiVersion: source.toolkit.fluxcd.io/v1                                      │
         │ kind: HelmRepository                                                         │
         │ metadata:                                                                    │
         │   name: easypj-private-charts                                                │
         │   namespace: flux-system                                                     │
         │ spec:                                                                        │
         │   url: https://charts.internal.yourorg.com                                   │
         │   interval: 1h                                                               │
         │   secretRef:                                                                 │
         │     name: private-chart-repo-creds                                           │
         │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
         │ # OCI Helm repository                                                        │
         │ apiVersion: source.toolkit.fluxcd.io/v1                                      │
         │ kind: HelmRepository                                                         │
         │ metadata:                                                                    │
         │   name: oci-charts                                                           │
         │   namespace: flux-system                                                     │
         │ spec:                                                                        │
         │   type: oci                                                                  │
         │   url: oci://registry.internal.yourorg.com/charts                            │
         │   interval: 1h                                                               │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    17.3 HelmRelease vs Kustomize
          - When to use Helm (complex apps, community charts)
          - When to use Kustomize (simple patches, overlays)
          - Combining both: Kustomize wrapping Helm
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │ START: "Should this easypj dependency use Helm or Kustomize?"                │
         │   │                                                                          │
         │   ├── Is it a THIRD-PARTY app (PostgreSQL, Redis, cert-manager)?             │
         │   │     ├── YES ─▶ Helm — you don't own the manifests; a chart               │
         │   │     │          gives you maintained defaults + easy upgrades             │
         │   │     └── NO  ─▶ continue                                                  │
         │   │                                                                          │
         │   ├── Is it easypj-web ITSELF — YOUR OWN Deployment/Service?                 │
         │   │     ├── YES ─▶ Kustomize — no templating engine needed for               │
         │   │     │          YAML you already fully control (Ch33.5)                   │
         │   │     └── NO  ─▶ continue                                                  │
         │   │                                                                          │
         │   └── Need to tweak ONE field on a third-party Helm chart's                  │
         │       OUTPUT without forking the chart?                                      │
         │         └──▶ COMBINE: Kustomize wraps the HelmRelease's                      │
         │              rendered output with a small strategic-merge patch              │
         │              — common for adding a label or annotation Bitnami's             │
         │              chart doesn't expose as a values.yaml field.                    │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    17.4 HelmRelease for easypj Stack
          - PostgreSQL via Bitnami Helm chart
          - Redis via Bitnami Helm chart
          - easypj via custom Helm chart
          - Complete Helm-based easypj deployment
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │ apiVersion: helm.toolkit.fluxcd.io/v2beta2                                   │
         │ kind: HelmRelease                                                            │
         │ metadata:                                                                    │
         │   name: postgres                                                             │
         │   namespace: easypj                                                          │
         │ spec:                                                                        │
         │   chart:                                                                     │
         │     spec:                                                                    │
         │       chart: postgresql                                                      │
         │       sourceRef: {kind: HelmRepository, name: bitnami}                       │
         │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
         │ apiVersion: helm.toolkit.fluxcd.io/v2beta2                                   │
         │ kind: HelmRelease                                                            │
         │ metadata:                                                                    │
         │   name: redis                                                                │
         │   namespace: easypj                                                          │
         │ spec:                                                                        │
         │   chart:                                                                     │
         │     spec: {chart: redis, sourceRef: {kind: HelmRepository, name: bitnami}}   │
         │   values:                                                                    │
         │     architecture: standalone                                                 │
         │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
         │ apiVersion: helm.toolkit.fluxcd.io/v2beta2                                   │
         │ kind: HelmRelease                                                            │
         │ metadata:                                                                    │
         │   name: easypj-web                                                           │
         │   namespace: easypj                                                          │
         │ spec:                                                                        │
         │   dependsOn:                                                                 │
         │     - name: postgres                                                         │
         │     - name: redis                                                            │
         │   chart:                                                                     │
         │     spec:                                                                    │
         │       chart: ./charts/easypj                                                 │
         │       sourceRef: {kind: GitRepository, name: easypj-config}                  │
         │                                                                              │
         │ 💡 dependsOn ensures postgres and redis are Ready BEFORE easypj-web's         │
         │    HelmRelease reconciles — the Helm equivalent of ArgoCD Sync Waves.        │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    🔧 UNDER THE HOOD — What Actually Happens When a HelmRelease Reconciles
    ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    The Helm Controller doesn't shell out to a helm binary behind the scenes
    — it uses Helm's own Go SDK directly, which means every HelmRelease
    reconciliation is the same install/upgrade logic the helm CLI uses, just
    triggered by a Kubernetes controller watching a CRD instead of a human
    running a command. That's why HelmRelease supports the exact same
    concepts CLI users know — values, releases, rollback, tests — but gets
    Flux's reconciliation loop on top for free: drift in a Helm-managed
    resource (someone manually edits a Deployment Helm created) gets
    detected and corrected exactly like any other Flux-managed resource.

    ⚠️ GOTCHAS
    ──────────
    • Putting a real secret value directly under values: in a HelmRelease
      — this is exactly as unsafe as a plaintext Kubernetes Secret in Git;
      use valuesFrom pointing at a Sealed Secret/ESO-managed Secret
      instead.
    • Assuming Kustomize and HelmRelease are mutually exclusive — the
      recommended pattern wraps a shared HelmRelease in per-environment
      Kustomize overlays to patch environment-specific values without
      duplicating the whole HelmRelease.
    • Setting upgrade.remediation.retries without remediateLastFailure —
      retries alone just tries the same failing upgrade again;
      remediateLastFailure is what actually triggers an automatic rollback
      to the last GOOD release.

    ⚡ SPEED TIPS
    ─────────────
    • HelmRelease = Flux's declarative replacement for helm install/helm
      upgrade — if a question describes managing Helm charts via a CRD,
      that's the HelmRelease.
    • valuesFrom + a Kubernetes Secret is the pattern for keeping
      credentials out of a HelmRelease's plaintext values block.
    • "Chart lives inside the same Git repo as the config" →
      sourceRef.kind: GitRepository. "Chart comes from Bitnami/a public
      registry" → sourceRef.kind: HelmRepository. Two source kinds, same
      HelmRelease shape.

    🔑 COMMAND REFERENCE
    ────────────────────
    ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │ flux create source helm bitnami --url=https://charts.bitnami.com/bitnami         │
    │     Register a HelmRepository source.                                            │
    │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
    │ flux get helmrelease -A                                                          │
    │     Check every HelmRelease's install/upgrade status across the cluster.         │
    └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    🏋️ PRACTICE DRILL — Fix a Leaking HelmRelease
    ───────────────────────────────────────────
    A teammate's HelmRelease for easypj-postgres has auth: {password:
    'supersecret123'} directly under values:, already committed and pushed.
    TASK:
       Describe the 2 things that must happen: how you'd fix the
       HelmRelease going forward (valuesFrom + which secret solution from
       Ch13), and why editing the file alone doesn't fully remediate this.

    📊 VISUAL SUMMARY
    ────────────────
    ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │ ▸ HelmRelease = declarative Helm                                     │
    │   Full lifecycle (install/upgrade/rollback/test) via CRD, using      │
    │   Helm's own SDK — not a wrapped CLI call.                           │
    │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
    │ ▸ 2 source kinds                                                     │
    │   GitRepository (chart lives in your repo) or HelmRepository         │
    │   (public/private chart repo, HTTP or OCI).                          │
    │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
    │ ▸ valuesFrom for secrets                                             │
    │   Pull sensitive values from a K8s Secret instead of hardcoding      │
    │   — pair with Sealed Secrets/ESO/SOPS from Ch13.                     │
    │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
    │ ▸ Kustomize wraps HelmRelease                                        │
    │   One shared HelmRelease + per-environment Kustomize patches =       │
    │   no duplicated YAML across dev/staging/prod.                        │
    └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    Chapter 17 — CGOA Exam Focus:
         🎯 HelmRelease CRD and its fields
         🎯 HelmRepository configuration
         🎯 Helm-based GitOps deployment


Chapter 18: FluxCD Image Automation
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    🟡 Intermediate · ⏱️ ~1 hour · 🎯 CGOA: FluxCD (20%) · 🏢 Daily-Work: The purest GitOps feature in this guide

    WHY THIS CHAPTER MATTERS
    ────────────────────────
    This chapter covers the single most CGOA-tested ArgoCD-vs-Flux contrast
    point in the entire exam — and, in the guide's own view, the most
    "correct" implementation of GitOps philosophy among every feature
    covered so far.

    🎯 LEARNING OBJECTIVES — by the end of this chapter you can:
    ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
      1. Name the 3 CRDs in Flux's image automation pipeline and what each
         does
      2. Explain why Flux commits to Git instead of patching the cluster
         directly
      3. Write a semver-range ImagePolicy that excludes pre-release tags
      4. State precisely what ArgoCD's Image Updater does differently

    📍 YOU ARE HERE
    ────────────────
    Part 5: FluxCD ▸ Chapter 3 of 5 ▸ Next: Chapter 19 (FluxCD Notifications and Monitoring)

    18.1 Image Automation Controller
          - Auto-detecting new container images
          - ImageRepository: tracking image tags
          - ImagePolicy: selecting versions
          [DIAGRAM: Image automation flow in Flux]

         [FLUX IMAGE AUTOMATION — auto-update from registry]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │                                                              │
         │  1. IMAGE REPOSITORY: Scan registry for new images           │
         │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐   │
         │  │  ImageRepository: ghcr.io/easypj/easypj              │   │
         │  │  Scans every 5 minutes                               │   │
         │  │  Finds: v1.0.0, v1.1.0, v2.0.0, v2.0.1, v2.1.0     │   │
         │  └──────────────────────┬───────────────────────────────┘   │
         │                         │                                     │
         │                         ▼                                     │
         │  2. IMAGE POLICY: Select which version to use                │
         │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐   │
         │  │  ImagePolicy: semver range 2.x.x                     │   │
         │  │  Filters: v2.0.0, v2.0.1, v2.1.0                     │   │
         │  │  Selects: v2.1.0 (highest in range)                  │   │
         │  └──────────────────────┬───────────────────────────────┘   │
         │                         │                                     │
         │                         ▼                                     │
         │  3. IMAGE UPDATE: Write new tag back to Git                  │
         │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐   │
         │  │  Flux commits to config repo:                        │   │
         │  │  "Update easypj image to v2.1.0"                     │   │
         │  │  → Kustomize/Helm picks up new version               │   │
         │  │  → Flux reconciliation triggers deploy               │   │
         │  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘   │
         │                                                              │
         │  💡 FULL GITOPS: Image update → Git commit → auto-deploy    │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    18.2 ImageRepository and ImagePolicy
          - Scanning container registries
          - Semver policy: only patch updates
          - Alphabetical, numerical, regex policies
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │ apiVersion: image.toolkit.fluxcd.io/v1beta2                                  │
         │ kind: ImageRepository                                                        │
         │ metadata:                                                                    │
         │   name: easypj-web                                                           │
         │   namespace: flux-system                                                     │
         │ spec:                                                                        │
         │   image: registry.yourorg.com/easypj/web                                     │
         │   interval: 5m                                                               │
         │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
         │ apiVersion: image.toolkit.fluxcd.io/v1beta2                                  │
         │ kind: ImagePolicy                                                            │
         │ metadata:                                                                    │
         │   name: easypj-web                                                           │
         │   namespace: flux-system                                                     │
         │ spec:                                                                        │
         │   imageRepositoryRef:                                                        │
         │     name: easypj-web                                                         │
         │   policy:                                                                    │
         │     semver:                                                                  │
         │       range: '>=2.0.0 <3.0.0'   # only patch/minor bumps, no v3 yet          │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    18.3 Image Update Automation
          - Automatically updating YAML in Git
          - Commit message templates
          - Push to config repo automatically
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │ apiVersion: image.toolkit.fluxcd.io/v1beta2                                  │
         │ kind: ImageUpdateAutomation                                                  │
         │ metadata:                                                                    │
         │   name: easypj-web                                                           │
         │   namespace: flux-system                                                     │
         │ spec:                                                                        │
         │   interval: 5m                                                               │
         │   sourceRef:                                                                 │
         │     kind: GitRepository                                                      │
         │     name: easypj-config                                                      │
         │   git:                                                                       │
         │     checkout: {ref: {branch: main}}                                          │
         │     commit:                                                                  │
         │       author: {email: flux@yourorg.com, name: fluxcdbot}                     │
         │       messageTemplate: |                                                     │
         │         Auto-update easypj-web to {{range .Updated.Images}}                  │
         │         {{println .}}{{end}}                                                 │
         │     push: {branch: main}                                                     │
         │   update:                                                                    │
         │     path: ./overlays/prod                                                    │
         │     strategy: Setters                                                        │
         │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
         │ EXPECTED: a new commit appears in easypj-config, authored by                 │
         │ fluxcdbot, updating the image tag — no human wrote this commit,              │
         │ but it's still a real, reviewable, revertable Git commit (Ch18.4).           │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    18.4 Flux Image Automation vs ArgoCD Image Updater
          [COMPARISON TABLE: Flux vs ArgoCD image automation]
          | Feature              | Flux Image Automation    | ArgoCD Image Updater   |
          | Push to Git          | ✅ Creates commits       | ❌ Direct to cluster    |
          | Semver Filtering     | ✅ ImagePolicy           | ✅ Update strategies    |
          | GitOps Principle     | ✅ Full GitOps (Git)     | ⚠️ Partial (cluster)   |

    🔧 UNDER THE HOOD — How Flux Finds the Exact Line to Change in Your YAML
    ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    ImageUpdateAutomation doesn't regenerate your whole deployment.yaml from
    a template — it uses a "Setters" strategy that looks for a specific
    marker comment (like # {"$imagepolicy": "flux-system:easypj-policy"})
    next to an image field, and does a surgical find-and-replace of just the
    tag on that one line. This is precisely why Flux's Git commits for image
    updates are minimal, single-line diffs instead of full-file rewrites —
    and why the marker comment is not optional decoration, it's the
    mechanism Flux uses to know WHERE to write, out of potentially hundreds
    of lines in your config repo.

    ⚠️ GOTCHAS
    ──────────
    • Forgetting the $imagepolicy marker comment entirely and wondering
      why ImageUpdateAutomation never commits anything — without the
      marker, Flux has no anchor to know which line to edit.
    • Assuming a semver ImagePolicy automatically picks pre-release tags
      like v2.2.0-rc1 — semver policies exclude pre-release/build metadata
      by default unless explicitly configured to include them.
    • Believing Flux's image automation and ArgoCD's Image Updater are
      functionally equivalent because both "auto-update images" — the
      target of the update (Git vs cluster) is the whole point of the
      distinction, not an implementation detail.

    ⚡ SPEED TIPS
    ─────────────
    • 3 CRDs, 3 jobs: ImageRepository=scan registry, ImagePolicy=pick a
      version, ImageUpdateAutomation=commit the change to Git.
    • "Commits to Git" = Flux. "Patches the cluster directly" = ArgoCD
      Image Updater. This exact phrase pairing is the fastest way to
      answer this comparison on the exam.
    • Push failure in ImageUpdateAutomation means NOTHING deploys — Flux
      retries next interval rather than falling back to a direct cluster
      patch. No image change ever bypasses Git.

    🔑 COMMAND REFERENCE
    ────────────────────
    ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │ flux create image policy easypj --image-ref=easypj --select-semver='>=1.0.0      │
    │ <3.0.0'                                                                          │
    │     Create an ImagePolicy filtering by semver range.                             │
    └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    🏋️ PRACTICE DRILL — Diagnose a Silent Image Automation
    ────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    You pushed easypj:v2.2.0 to the registry 20 minutes ago. Nothing has
    changed in the config repo and the cluster is still running v2.1.0.
    TASK:
       List the 2 most likely causes (checking ImageRepository/ImagePolicy
       status first, then the marker comment) before assuming Flux itself
       is broken.

    📊 VISUAL SUMMARY
    ────────────────
    ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │ ▸ 3-stage pipeline                                                   │
    │   ImageRepository (scan) → ImagePolicy (select) →                    │
    │   ImageUpdateAutomation (commit to Git).                             │
    │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
    │ ▸ Pure GitOps                                                        │
    │   Flux commits the new tag to Git — full audit trail, git            │
    │   revert works, no bypass.                                           │
    │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
    │ ▸ vs ArgoCD Image Updater                                            │
    │   Patches the cluster directly, no Git commit — faster but           │
    │   breaks the audit trail; a favorite exam contrast.                  │
    │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
    │ ▸ Setters strategy                                                   │
    │   Flux edits YAML surgically via a marker comment, not a             │
    │   full-file regeneration — minimal, clean diffs.                     │
    └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    Chapter 18 — CGOA Exam Focus:
         🎯 Image automation concept
         🎯 ImageRepository and ImagePolicy CRDs
         🎯 Git-based image updates vs cluster-direct


Chapter 19: FluxCD Notifications and Monitoring
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    🟡 Intermediate · ⏱️ ~1 hour · 🎯 CGOA: FluxCD (20%) · 🏢 Daily-Work: Mirrors Chapter 11, Flux-flavored

    WHY THIS CHAPTER MATTERS
    ────────────────────────
    If Chapter 11 was ArgoCD's visibility layer, this is Flux's — same
    underlying need (know when something breaks, fix latency with webhooks),
    different CRDs and a different metrics surface to query.

    🎯 LEARNING OBJECTIVES — by the end of this chapter you can:
    ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
      1. Configure a Provider + Alert pair that notifies only on failures
      2. Explain what eventSeverity: error filters out compared to info
      3. Set up a webhook Receiver for instant reconciliation
      4. Read a Flux health status (Ready/Not Ready/Failed/Suspended/Drift
         Detected) and know the next action for each

    📍 YOU ARE HERE
    ────────────────
    Part 5: FluxCD ▸ Chapter 4 of 5 ▸ Next: Chapter 20 (FluxCD vs ArgoCD — Making the Choice)

    19.1 Flux Notification Controller
          - Providers: Slack, Discord, Teams, GitHub, GitLab
          - Alert CRD: event severity filtering
          - Provider CRD: where to send notifications
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │ apiVersion: notification.toolkit.fluxcd.io/v1beta3                           │
         │ kind: Provider                                                               │
         │ metadata:                                                                    │
         │   name: slack                                                                │
         │   namespace: flux-system                                                     │
         │ spec:                                                                        │
         │   type: slack                                                                │
         │   channel: gitops-alerts                                                     │
         │   secretRef:                                                                 │
         │     name: slack-webhook-url                                                  │
         │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
         │ apiVersion: notification.toolkit.fluxcd.io/v1beta3                           │
         │ kind: Alert                                                                  │
         │ metadata:                                                                    │
         │   name: easypj-alerts                                                        │
         │   namespace: flux-system                                                     │
         │ spec:                                                                        │
         │   providerRef:                                                               │
         │     name: slack                                                              │
         │   eventSeverity: info    # 'error' would filter out success events           │
         │   eventSources:                                                              │
         │     - kind: Kustomization                                                    │
         │       name: easypj                                                           │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    19.2 Flux Monitoring with Prometheus
          - Built-in metrics endpoints
          - Flux Grafana dashboards
          - Key metrics: reconciliation duration, error rates
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │ apiVersion: monitoring.coreos.com/v1                                         │
         │ kind: ServiceMonitor                                                         │
         │ metadata:                                                                    │
         │   name: flux-system                                                          │
         │   namespace: flux-system                                                     │
         │ spec:                                                                        │
         │   selector:                                                                  │
         │     matchLabels: {app: source-controller}                                    │
         │   endpoints:                                                                 │
         │     - port: http-prom                                                        │
         │       interval: 30s                                                          │
         │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
         │ KEY METRICS EXPOSED (all /metrics endpoints, Prometheus format):             │
         │   gotk_reconcile_duration_seconds   — how long each reconcile took           │
         │   gotk_reconcile_condition          — current Ready/Failed status            │
         │   controller_runtime_reconcile_errors_total — error counter                  │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    19.3 Flux Webhook Receivers
          - Instant reconciliation via webhooks
          - GitHub/GitLab webhook setup
          - Reducing polling interval via webhooks
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │ apiVersion: notification.toolkit.fluxcd.io/v1                                │
         │ kind: Receiver                                                               │
         │ metadata:                                                                    │
         │   name: easypj-config-webhook                                                │
         │   namespace: flux-system                                                     │
         │ spec:                                                                        │
         │   type: github                                                               │
         │   events: ['push']                                                           │
         │   secretRef:                                                                 │
         │     name: webhook-token                                                      │
         │   resources:                                                                 │
         │     - kind: GitRepository                                                    │
         │       name: easypj-config                                                    │
         │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
         │ $ kubectl get receiver -n flux-system easypj-config-webhook \                │
         │     -o jsonpath='{.status.webhookPath}'                                      │
         │ # -> /hook/3f6a8d... — add THIS path to GitHub's webhook Payload URL         │
         │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
         │ Same event-driven pattern as Ch11's ArgoCD webhook (Ch33.1): a push          │
         │ triggers immediate reconciliation instead of waiting for the next            │
         │ GitRepository poll interval.                                                 │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    19.4 Flux Health Assessment
          - Health checks on reconciled resources
          - kstatus and ready checks
          - Custom health checks
          [TABLE: Flux health status values]

    🔧 UNDER THE HOOD — Why Flux Split Provider From Alert Instead of One Combined Object
    ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    ArgoCD's notification config bundles trigger, template, and destination
    into one interconnected ConfigMap. Flux deliberately splits this into
    two separate CRDs — Provider (WHERE, e.g. this Slack webhook) and Alert
    (WHEN, e.g. errors from any Kustomization) — so that one Provider can be
    reused by many Alerts, and one Alert's severity/source filtering can be
    tuned without touching where it sends to. This is the same
    micro-controller philosophy as Chapter 16 applied to notifications
    specifically: smaller, independently-reconciled objects instead of one
    large configuration blob.

    ⚠️ GOTCHAS
    ──────────
    • Setting eventSeverity: info on a busy production channel — this is
      the FluxCD equivalent of Chapter 11's notification-flooding gotcha;
      scope to error for any channel humans are expected to act on.
    • Forgetting the webhook secret must match between the Git provider's
      webhook settings and Flux's Receiver — a mismatch fails silently,
      same failure mode as ArgoCD's webhook gotcha from Chapter 11.
    • Treating "Drift Detected" as an error state requiring action — it's
      actually evidence Flux's self-healing IS working; the drift gets
      corrected automatically on the next reconciliation, no human
      intervention needed.

    ⚡ SPEED TIPS
    ─────────────
    • Provider = WHERE (Slack/Discord/Teams/GitHub). Alert = WHEN
      (severity + which Kustomizations/HelmReleases to watch) — two CRDs,
      cleanly separated.
    • 5 Flux health states to know cold: Ready, Not Ready, Failed,
      Suspended, Drift Detected — "Drift Detected" is not a failure, it's
      self-healing in progress.
    • flux suspend / flux resume is the Flux-native way to pause
      reconciliation for maintenance — the direct equivalent of pausing an
      ArgoCD auto-sync.

    🔑 COMMAND REFERENCE
    ────────────────────
    ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │ flux create alert on-call --provider=slack --event-severity=error                │
    │     Create an Alert that only fires on errors.                                   │
    │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
    │ flux suspend kustomization <name>                                                │
    │     Pause reconciliation for planned maintenance.                                │
    └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    🏋️ PRACTICE DRILL — Split Provider From Alert
    ───────────────────────────────────────────
    easypj's team wants Slack alerts on errors for their #deploys channel,
    AND wants a separate #audit channel that logs literally every
    reconciliation event, success or failure.
    TASK:
       Describe the Provider(s) and Alert(s) you'd create — how many of
       each, and what eventSeverity each Alert uses.

    📊 VISUAL SUMMARY
    ────────────────
    ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │ ▸ Provider vs Alert                                                  │
    │   Provider = WHERE to send. Alert = WHEN to send (severity +         │
    │   source). Independently reusable and tunable.                       │
    │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
    │ ▸ 5 health states                                                    │
    │   Ready, Not Ready, Failed, Suspended, Drift Detected — Drift        │
    │   Detected means self-healing is actively working.                   │
    │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
    │ ▸ Webhook receivers                                                  │
    │   Same latency fix as ArgoCD's — instant reconciliation instead      │
    │   of waiting for the poll interval.                                  │
    │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
    │ ▸ flux suspend/resume                                                │
    │   Pause/resume reconciliation for maintenance windows — Flux's       │
    │   equivalent of disabling auto-sync.                                 │
    └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    Chapter 19 — CGOA Exam Focus:
         🎯 Notification providers and Alerts
         🎯 Webhook receivers
         🎯 Health assessment in Flux


Chapter 20: FluxCD vs ArgoCD — Making the Choice
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    🟡 Intermediate · ⏱️ ~1 hour · 🎯 CGOA: ArgoCD/FluxCD comparison (spans both domains) · 🏢 Daily-Work: The decision you make once, live with for years

    WHY THIS CHAPTER MATTERS
    ────────────────────────
    This chapter is the synthesis of everything in Parts 3 and 5 — nearly
    every CGOA question that mentions BOTH tools in the same sentence is
    testing something from this chapter's comparison table, not new
    material.

    🎯 LEARNING OBJECTIVES — by the end of this chapter you can:
    ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
      1. Recite the 5 sharpest ArgoCD-vs-FluxCD contrasts from memory (UI,
         architecture, Helm, image automation, SOPS)
      2. Recommend a tool for a described team profile and justify it
      3. Design a coexistence setup where both tools manage different
         parts of one cluster
      4. Describe what changes (and what doesn't) when migrating an app
         from one tool to the other

    📍 YOU ARE HERE
    ────────────────
    Part 5: FluxCD ▸ Chapter 5 of 5 ▸ Next: Part 6 — Advanced GitOps Patterns (Chapter 21)

    20.1 Detailed Comparison
          [COMPARISON TABLE: Comprehensive Flux vs ArgoCD]
          | Aspect               | ArgoCD                      | FluxCD                      |
          | UI                   | Excellent web dashboard     | CLI-focused, basic UI       |
          | Architecture         | Monolithic server           | Micro-controllers           |
          | Dependencies         | Redis, Repo Server          | Each controller standalone  |
          | Multi-tenancy        | Projects with RBAC          | Cross-namespace references  |
          | Helm                 | Native via source           | HelmRelease CRD             |
          | Image Automation     | Separate Image Updater      | Built-in Image Automation   |
          | SOPS                 | Via plugin                  | Native support              |
          | GitOps Engine        | Custom reconciliation       | Controller-runtime based    |
          | Learning Curve       | Moderate (UI helps)         | Steeper (YAML-heavy)        |
          | Enterprise Features  | SSO, RBAC, Projects         | Multi-tenancy, Notification |

    20.2 When to Choose ArgoCD
          - Teams that want a rich web UI
          - Multiple teams/projects on same cluster
          - Need SSO and fine-grained RBAC
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │ ▸ Multi-team platform, one shared cluster                                    │
         │   Projects + RBAC give clean tenant isolation without N clusters             │
         │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
         │ ▸ Non-Kubernetes-expert stakeholders need visibility                         │
         │   The rich web UI (resource tree, live diffs) is self-service —              │
         │   fewer "can you check the status of X" Slack pings                          │
         │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
         │ ▸ SSO/enterprise auth is a hard requirement                                  │
         │   OIDC/SAML integration is a first-class ArgoCD feature                      │
         │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
         │ ▸ Team is new to GitOps                                                      │
         │   The UI's visual sync/diff/rollback lowers the learning curve               │
         │   vs. reasoning about CLI output alone                                       │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    20.3 When to Choose FluxCD
          - GitOps purists (everything from Git)
          - Already using GitOps Toolkit
          - Strong preference for Kustomize + SOPS
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │ ▸ "GitOps purist" philosophy — everything reconciled, nothing                │
         │   clicked                                                                    │
         │   No UI to lean on means the team is forced to keep Git as the               │
         │   actual source of truth, not a UI-driven workaround                         │
         │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
         │ ▸ Heavy Kustomize users                                                      │
         │   Native Kustomize Controller vs. ArgoCD's more bolted-on support            │
         │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
         │ ▸ Native secrets encryption matters                                          │
         │   SOPS decryption built in, no plugin to maintain                            │
         │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
         │ ▸ Resource-constrained clusters (edge, small footprint)                      │
         │   Micro-controller architecture — Prometheus Controller Manager              │
         │   overhead is smaller than ArgoCD's Redis + Repo Server + API                │
         │   Server + Application Controller footprint                                  │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    20.4 Can You Use Both?
          - Coexistence patterns
          - Separation by namespace/cluster
          - Migration between tools
          [DIAGRAM: Coexistence architecture]

          [ARGOCD + FLUX — coexistence pattern]
          ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
          │  KUBERNETES CLUSTER                                           │
          │  ┌─────────────────────────┐  ┌─────────────────────────┐   │
          │  │  argocd namespace        │  │  flux-system namespace   │   │
          │  │  ┌─────────────────────┐ │  │  ┌─────────────────────┐ │   │
          │  │  │ ArgoCD controllers  │ │  │  │ Flux controllers    │ │   │
          │  │  └──────────┬──────────┘ │  │  └──────────┬──────────┘ │   │
          │  │             │            │  │             │            │   │
          │  │             ▼            │  │             ▼            │   │
          │  │  Manages:               │  │  Manages:               │   │
          │  │  • easypj (main app)    │  │  • Monitoring stack      │   │
          │  │  • postgres (database)  │  │  • Logging (Loki/ELK)   │   │
          │  │  • redis (cache)        │  │  • Cert-manager          │   │
          │  └─────────────────────────┘  └─────────────────────────┘   │
          │                                                              │
          │  💡 Use ArgoCD for application workloads (rich UI)           │
          │  💡 Use Flux for infrastructure add-ons (SOPS native)        │
          │  💡 Both read from the SAME Git config repo!                 │
          └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    20.5 Which Should easypj Use?
          - Decision framework based on team size
          - Recommended path: Start with ArgoCD (UI helps learning)
          - Advanced path: FluxCD for GitOps purists
          [DECISION MATRIX for easypj]

          [DECISION MATRIX — easypj specific]
          ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
          │                                                              │
          │  easypj TEAM PROFILE:                                        │
          │  • Small team (2-5 developers)                               │
          │  • Django app (Python)                                       │
          │  • Need: dev/staging/production environments                 │
          │  • Want: easy visibility into deployments                    │
          │                                                              │
          │  RECOMMENDATION: START WITH ARGOCD ✅                         │
          │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐   │
          │  │  WHY:                                                 │   │
          │  │  1. Rich UI = easier for team to adopt                │   │
          │  │  2. Shows sync status visually (green=good, red=bad)  │   │
          │  │  3. Easy to create Applications via UI or CLI         │   │
          │  │  4. ApplicationSets simplify multi-environment        │   │
          │  │  5. Large community, many tutorials                   │   │
          │  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘   │
          │                                                              │
          │  LATER: CONSIDER FLUXCD FOR ADVANCED USE CASES               │
          │  • When you need SOPS for secret encryption                  │
          │  • When you want image automation with Git commits           │
          │  • When you prefer micro-controller architecture             │
          │                                                              │
          └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    🔧 UNDER THE HOOD — Why Coexistence Doesn't Cause Conflicts
    ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    ArgoCD and FluxCD can run on the same cluster without stepping on each
    other because neither one claims ownership of a resource it didn't
    create — each only reconciles what its OWN
    Applications/Kustomizations/HelmReleases point to. As long as the two
    tools are pointed at non-overlapping sets of resources (Chapter 20's
    example: ArgoCD owns the app + database, Flux owns monitoring +
    cert-manager), there's no fight over drift correction, because neither
    tool ever sees the other's resources as "its" desired state to reconcile
    against. The one thing that WOULD break this is pointing both tools at
    the SAME resource — that genuinely does cause a fight, each one
    reverting the other's changes on its own reconciliation interval.

    ⚠️ GOTCHAS
    ──────────
    • Assuming migration between tools means a total rewrite — Flux
      Kustomizations and ArgoCD Applications both point at the same Git
      paths; migrating is largely a matter of swapping the operator's CRD,
      not restructuring the config repo.
    • Picking a tool based on "which is more popular" rather than the
      team's actual constraints (UI need, SOPS requirement, multi-tenancy
      need) — Chapter 20.5's decision matrix exists specifically to
      replace popularity with fit.
    • Running both tools pointed at the SAME resources "just to be safe" —
      this is the one coexistence mistake that causes real conflicts (see
      Under the Hood).

    ⚡ SPEED TIPS
    ─────────────
    • 5-point comparison, memorize as pairs: UI (Argo rich / Flux CLI),
      Architecture (Argo monolith / Flux micro-controllers), Helm (Argo
      native source / Flux HelmRelease), Image automation (Argo
      cluster-direct / Flux Git-commit), SOPS (Argo via plugin / Flux
      native).
    • "Small team wants visual feedback and easy onboarding" = ArgoCD.
      "GitOps purist, already Kustomize-heavy, needs native SOPS" =
      FluxCD. Team-profile language is the signal.
    • Coexistence is safe as long as the two tools' managed resources
      don't overlap — that one sentence answers most coexistence
      questions.

    🔑 COMMAND REFERENCE
    ────────────────────
    None yet — this chapter is conceptual. Your first real
    commands appear in the next hands-on chapter.

    🏋️ PRACTICE DRILL — Recommend a Tool and Defend It
    ────────────────────────────────────────────────
    A 15-person platform team already runs FluxCD for infrastructure add-ons
    (cert-manager, monitoring) and now needs to onboard 8 application teams
    who want to see their own deployment status without asking platform for
    help.
    TASK:
       Recommend ArgoCD, FluxCD, or both (coexisting) for the new
       application workloads specifically, and justify using exactly one
       fact from this chapter's comparison table.

    📊 VISUAL SUMMARY
    ────────────────
    ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │ ▸ 5 sharpest contrasts                                               │
    │   UI, architecture, Helm handling, image automation direction,       │
    │   SOPS support — memorize these 5 first.                             │
    │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
    │ ▸ Team-profile shortcut                                              │
    │   Rich-UI/multi-team/SSO need → ArgoCD.                              │
    │   GitOps-purist/Kustomize-heavy/native-SOPS need → FluxCD.           │
    │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
    │ ▸ Coexistence is safe                                                │
    │   As long as each tool manages a non-overlapping set of              │
    │   resources — same Git repo, different paths, no fights.             │
    │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
