Chapter 21: Progressive Delivery with GitOps
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    🔴 Advanced · ⏱️ ~1.5 hours · 🎯 CGOA: Practice & Security (20%) · 🏢 Daily-Work: Reducing blast radius of bad deploys

    WHY THIS CHAPTER MATTERS
    ────────────────────────
    Everything before Part 6 deploys 100% of traffic to a new version the
    moment ArgoCD/Flux syncs — this chapter is where GitOps stops being
    all-or-nothing and starts limiting how many easypj users a bad release
    can reach before it's caught.

    🎯 LEARNING OBJECTIVES — by the end of this chapter you can:
    ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
      1. Rank rolling update, blue-green, and canary by resource cost and
         rollback speed
      2. Write a Rollout resource with a 4-step canary strategy
      3. Explain what triggers an automated canary abort
      4. Identify whether ArgoCD (Argo Rollouts) or FluxCD (Flagger) is
         being described in a scenario

    📍 YOU ARE HERE
    ────────────────
    Part 6: Advanced GitOps Patterns ▸ Chapter 1 of 4 ▸ Next: Chapter 22 (GitOps with Service Mesh)

    21.1 What is Progressive Delivery?
          - Beyond basic deployment: canary, blue-green
          - Progressive delivery vs continuous delivery
          - How GitOps enables progressive delivery
          [DIAGRAM: Progressive delivery strategies spectrum]

         [PROGRESSIVE DELIVERY — deployment strategies compared]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  ROLLING UPDATE (basic):                                     │
         │  Old pods ████████░░░░░░░░ New pods  (gradual replacement)  │
         │                                                              │
         │  BLUE-GREEN:                                                 │
         │  🔵 BLUE (active) → 🟢 GREEN (new) → traffic switch        │
         │  ┌──────────┐    ┌──────────┐                               │
         │  │Blue (v1) │───▶│Green (v2)│  Instant cutover              │
         │  │3 pods    │    │3 pods    │  Instant rollback              │
         │  └──────────┘    └──────────┘                               │
         │                                                              │
         │  CANARY:                                                     │
         │  v1 ████████████████████░ v2 (5% → 25% → 50% → 100%)       │
         │  Gradual traffic shift + metric monitoring + auto-rollback   │
         │  ✅ Safest approach for production                            │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    21.2 Argo Rollouts
          - Argo Rollouts CRD and controller
          - Canary deployments with traffic splitting
          - Blue-green deployments with automated promotion
          - Integration with Service Mesh (Istio, Linkerd)
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │ apiVersion: argoproj.io/v1alpha1                                             │
         │ kind: Rollout                                                                │
         │ metadata:                                                                    │
         │   name: easypj-web                                                           │
         │   namespace: easypj                                                          │
         │ spec:                                                                        │
         │   replicas: 5                                                                │
         │   strategy:                                                                  │
         │     canary:                                                                  │
         │       steps:                                                                 │
         │         - setWeight: 20                                                      │
         │         - pause: {duration: 5m}                                              │
         │         - analysis:                                                          │
         │             templates:                                                       │
         │               - templateName: success-rate                                   │
         │         - setWeight: 50                                                      │
         │         - pause: {duration: 5m}                                              │
         │         - setWeight: 100                                                     │
         │   selector:                                                                  │
         │     matchLabels: {app: easypj-web}                                           │
         │   template:                                                                  │
         │     metadata:                                                                │
         │       labels: {app: easypj-web}                                              │
         │     spec:                                                                    │
         │       containers:                                                            │
         │         - name: easypj                                                       │
         │           image: registry.yourorg.com/easypj/web:v2.0.0                      │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    21.3 Canary Deployments with Argo Rollouts
          - Gradual traffic shifting (10% → 25% → 50% → 100%)
          - Automated analysis with metrics
          - Auto-rollback on failure
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │ T+0min   setWeight: 20   ██░░░░░░░░  v1=80% v2=20%                           │
         │                           AnalysisRun starts: checking success-rate          │
         │ T+5min   analysis PASSES   → auto-advance to next step                       │
         │ T+5min   setWeight: 50   █████░░░░░  v1=50% v2=50%                           │
         │ T+10min  analysis PASSES   → auto-advance                                    │
         │ T+10min  setWeight: 100  ██████████  v1=0%  v2=100%                          │
         │ T+10min  🟢 Promotion complete — v2 is now fully live                         │
         │                                                                              │
         │ IF ANALYSIS FAILS AT ANY STEP:                                               │
         │ T+Xmin   error-rate exceeds threshold → 🔴 AUTO-ROLLBACK                      │
         │          weight reverts to 0%, v1 stays fully serving traffic —              │
         │          no human had to notice or react                                     │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    21.4 Blue-Green Deployments with Argo Rollouts
          - Two complete environments (blue=current, green=new)
          - Traffic switch via Service selector
          - Automated promotion after health check
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │ BEFORE PROMOTION                                                             │
         │   Service selector: version=blue  ──▶  BLUE (v1): 5 pods, 100% traffic       │
         │                                        GREEN (v2): 5 pods, 0% traffic,       │
         │                                                     already health-checked   │
         │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
         │ PROMOTION EVENT                                                              │
         │   Service selector flips: version=blue  ──▶  version=green                   │
         │   (one field change — instant, no new pods created or destroyed)             │
         │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
         │ AFTER PROMOTION                                                              │
         │   Service selector: version=green  ──▶  GREEN (v2): 5 pods, 100%             │
         │                                                      traffic (promoted)      │
         │                                         BLUE (v1): 5 pods, 0% traffic        │
         │                                                     (kept warm for rollback) │
         │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
         │ 💡 Unlike canary, BOTH versions run at FULL scale simultaneously —            │
         │    the switch is instant (just a Service selector change), and               │
         │    rollback is equally instant: flip the selector back to blue.              │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    21.5 Progressive Delivery in Flux (Flagger)
          - Flagger: progressive delivery for Flux
          - Canary CRD and automated analysis
          - Integration with Linkerd, Istio, Contour
          [CODE BLOCK: Flagger Canary for easypj]

    🔧 UNDER THE HOOD — Why a Rollout Isn't Just a Fancier Deployment
    ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    A Kubernetes Deployment has no concept of "pause here and check metrics"
    — its rolling update is a fixed, uninterruptible ramp. Argo Rollouts'
    Rollout CRD is a genuinely different controller that manages ReplicaSets
    the way a Deployment does, but adds a state machine on top: setWeight
    steps, pause windows, and — critically — an AnalysisRun that queries
    Prometheus (or another metrics provider) between steps and can
    autonomously abort by scaling the canary ReplicaSet back to zero. This
    is why "just use a Deployment with more replicas" isn't progressive
    delivery: nothing is watching metrics or making a promote/abort decision
    without a human, which is the entire point of this chapter.

    ⚠️ GOTCHAS
    ──────────
    • Assuming blue-green is always safer than canary because it's an
      instant switch — it's instant in BOTH directions, meaning a bad
      release also reaches 100% of users instantly if promoted without
      adequate verification; canary's gradual exposure is what actually
      limits blast radius.
    • Forgetting blue-green needs 2x the resources during cutover — budget
      for this before choosing it over canary for a resource-constrained
      cluster.
    • Confusing Argo Rollouts (ArgoCD's progressive delivery tool) with
      Flagger (FluxCD's) — they solve the same problem with different CRDs
      (Rollout vs Canary) and different ecosystems; a question naming one
      implies the other tool's operator isn't in play.

    ⚡ SPEED TIPS
    ─────────────
    • Cost/speed pairing: Rolling=cheap/slow-rollback,
      Blue-Green=2x-cost/instant-rollback,
      Canary=normal-cost/instant-automated-rollback. Canary is the
      CGOA-favored default for production.
    • Argo Rollouts = ArgoCD's tool (Rollout CRD). Flagger = FluxCD's tool
      (Canary CRD). Matching tool-to-ecosystem is a frequent exam pairing.
    • "Automated rollback on metric degradation" in a question stem always
      means progressive delivery is in play — check whether it's asking
      about Argo Rollouts or Flagger based on which GitOps tool the
      scenario uses.

    🔑 COMMAND REFERENCE
    ────────────────────
    ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │ kubectl argo rollouts get rollout <name> --watch                                 │
    │     Watch a canary's live promotion progress.                                    │
    │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
    │ kubectl argo rollouts promote <name>                                             │
    │     Manually advance a paused canary to its next step.                           │
    └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    🏋️ PRACTICE DRILL — Choose a Deployment Strategy
    ──────────────────────────────────────────────
    easypj is about to ship a database schema change alongside application
    code — a change that's risky to run partially (some pods on old schema,
    some on new).
    TASK:
       Recommend rolling update, blue-green, or canary for this specific
       release, and explain the ONE property of your chosen strategy that
       makes it fit a schema-change scenario better than the other two.

    📊 VISUAL SUMMARY
    ────────────────
    ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │ ▸ 3 strategies, 1 spectrum                                           │
    │   Rolling (cheap, slow) → Canary (normal cost,                       │
    │   gradual+automated) → Blue-Green (2x cost, instant both ways).      │
    │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
    │ ▸ Rollout ≠ Deployment                                               │
    │   Adds a state machine: setWeight steps, pauses, and                 │
    │   AnalysisRun-driven auto-abort — no human required to catch a       │
    │   bad canary.                                                        │
    │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
    │ ▸ Argo Rollouts vs Flagger                                           │
    │   ArgoCD's canary tool vs FluxCD's — same concept                    │
    │   (Canary/Rollout CRD), different ecosystem.                         │
    │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
    │ ▸ Auto-rollback trigger                                              │
    │   AnalysisRun metrics breach threshold → automatic abort, scale      │
    │   canary to zero, no human in the loop.                              │
    └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    Chapter 21 — CGOA Exam Focus:
         🎯 Progressive delivery concepts
         🎯 Argo Rollouts basics
         🎯 Canary vs blue-green strategies


Chapter 22: GitOps with Service Mesh
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    🔴 Advanced · ⏱️ ~1 hour · 🎯 CGOA: Practice & Security (20%) · 🏢 Daily-Work: Traffic rules as reviewable YAML

    WHY THIS CHAPTER MATTERS
    ────────────────────────
    Progressive delivery in Chapter 21 needs SOMETHING to actually split
    traffic between versions — a service mesh is that something, and putting
    its config in Git means a canary's traffic percentages go through the
    exact same PR review as everything else.

    🎯 LEARNING OBJECTIVES — by the end of this chapter you can:
    ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
      1. Explain what mTLS, traffic splitting, and observability each
         solve
      2. Distinguish what a VirtualService controls from what a
         DestinationRule controls
      3. Choose Istio or Linkerd for a described team's complexity
         tolerance
      4. Trace how a canary's traffic percentage change becomes a
         Git-reviewable PR

    📍 YOU ARE HERE
    ────────────────
    Part 6: Advanced GitOps Patterns ▸ Chapter 2 of 4 ▸ Next: Chapter 23 (GitOps for Infrastructure as Code)

    22.1 Service Mesh and GitOps — Natural Partners
          - What a service mesh does (mTLS, traffic splitting, observability)
          - Why GitOps is ideal for managing service mesh config
          - Service mesh configuration as code in Git
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │ config repo (Git)                                                      │
         │   virtualservice.yaml, destinationrule.yaml   ──▶  ArgoCD / Flux       │
         │                                                         │              │
         │                                                         ▼              │
         │                                           kubectl apply (just CRDs)    │
         │                                                         │              │
         │                                                         ▼              │
         │                                     ┌───────────────────────────┐      │
         │                                     │  istiod / linkerd control  │     │
         │                                     │  plane reads the CRDs      │     │
         │                                     └──────────────┬─────────────┘     │
         │                                                     │ pushes config    │
         │                                                     ▼                  │
         │                         ┌─────────────┐      ┌─────────────┐           │
         │                         │ easypj-web  │      │ easypj-web  │           │
         │                         │ pod (v1)    │      │ pod (v2)    │           │
         │                         │ +Envoy/     │      │ +Envoy/     │           │
         │                         │ linkerd-    │      │ linkerd-    │           │
         │                         │ proxy       │      │ proxy       │           │
         │                         │ sidecar     │      │ sidecar     │           │
         │                         └─────────────┘      └─────────────┘           │
         │                          90% traffic          10% traffic              │
         │                                                                        │
         │ 💡 ArgoCD/Flux never talk to istiod directly — they only apply the      │
         │    VirtualService/DestinationRule YAML. The mesh control plane         │
         │    watches those CRDs itself and reconfigures the sidecars.            │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    22.2 Istio + GitOps
          - Managing Istio configurations via Git
          - VirtualService, DestinationRule in Git
          - Traffic splitting for canary with GitOps
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │ apiVersion: networking.istio.io/v1beta1                                │
         │ kind: VirtualService                                                   │
         │ metadata:                                                              │
         │   name: easypj-web                                                     │
         │   namespace: easypj                                                    │
         │ spec:                                                                  │
         │   hosts:                                                               │
         │     - easypj-web                                                       │
         │   http:                                                                │
         │     - route:                                                           │
         │         - destination:                                                 │
         │             host: easypj-web                                           │
         │             subset: v1                                                 │
         │           weight: 90                                                   │
         │         - destination:                                                 │
         │             host: easypj-web                                           │
         │             subset: v2                                                 │
         │           weight: 10                                                   │
         │ ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
         │ apiVersion: networking.istio.io/v1beta1                                │
         │ kind: DestinationRule                                                  │
         │ metadata:                                                              │
         │   name: easypj-web                                                     │
         │   namespace: easypj                                                    │
         │ spec:                                                                  │
         │   host: easypj-web                                                     │
         │   subsets:                                                             │
         │     - name: v1                                                         │
         │       labels:                                                          │
         │         version: v1                                                    │
         │     - name: v2                                                         │
         │       labels:                                                          │
         │         version: v2                                                    │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    22.3 Linkerd + GitOps
          - Linkerd installation via GitOps (ArgoCD/Flux)
          - Managing service profiles and retries
          - Automatic mTLS with GitOps
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │ apiVersion: helm.toolkit.fluxcd.io/v2beta2                             │
         │ kind: HelmRelease                                                      │
         │ metadata:                                                              │
         │   name: linkerd-control-plane                                          │
         │   namespace: linkerd                                                   │
         │ spec:                                                                  │
         │   chart:                                                               │
         │     spec:                                                              │
         │       chart: linkerd-control-plane                                     │
         │       sourceRef:                                                       │
         │         kind: HelmRepository                                           │
         │         name: linkerd                                                  │
         │ ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
         │ apiVersion: linkerd.io/v1alpha2                                        │
         │ kind: ServiceProfile                                                   │
         │ metadata:                                                              │
         │   name: easypj-web.easypj.svc.cluster.local                            │
         │   namespace: easypj                                                    │
         │ spec:                                                                  │
         │   routes:                                                              │
         │     - name: GET /api/jobs                                              │
         │       condition:                                                       │
         │         method: GET                                                    │
         │         pathRegex: /api/jobs                                           │
         │       retryBudget:                                                     │
         │         retryRatio: 0.2                                                │
         │         minRetriesPerSecond: 10                                        │
         │         ttl: 10s                                                       │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    🔧 UNDER THE HOOD — Why Mesh Config Is "Just Another CRD" to ArgoCD and Flux
    ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    Neither ArgoCD nor Flux has any special-case logic for Istio or Linkerd
    resources — a VirtualService, DestinationRule, or ServiceProfile is just
    another Kubernetes custom resource, reconciled through the exact same
    Application/Kustomization mechanism as a Deployment or ConfigMap. This
    is precisely why service mesh configuration slots into GitOps so
    cleanly: there's nothing mesh-specific to learn about ArgoCD or Flux
    themselves, only the mesh's own CRDs. The one place this DOES get more
    interesting is Argo Rollouts/Flagger, which actively read and rewrite a
    VirtualService's traffic weights as part of an automated canary — that's
    the one integration point where progressive delivery and service mesh
    config genuinely talk to each other.

    ⚠️ GOTCHAS
    ──────────
    • Assuming a service mesh is required for canary deployments — Argo
      Rollouts can shift traffic via Service selectors/weighted
      ReplicaSets without a mesh; a mesh adds finer-grained control
      (header-based routing, mTLS) but isn't a hard prerequisite.
    • Choosing Istio by default "because it's more popular" — Linkerd's
      zero-config mTLS and lower resource footprint is frequently the
      better fit for teams that don't need Istio's advanced
      traffic-shaping features.
    • Forgetting that mTLS being "automatic" in Linkerd still requires the
      mesh itself to be installed and each namespace to have the proxy
      injected — automatic means no manual certificate config, not zero
      setup.

    ⚡ SPEED TIPS
    ─────────────
    • mTLS = encryption. Traffic splitting = canary/A-B routing.
      Observability = latency/error visibility. Three distinct jobs a mesh
      does — don't conflate them in an answer.
    • VirtualService = routing RULES (which subset gets what %).
      DestinationRule = subset DEFINITIONS (what "v1"/"v2" even means,
      plus TLS/load-balancing policy). VirtualService references
      DestinationRule's subsets.
    • Istio = powerful but complex (many CRDs). Linkerd = simple,
      auto-mTLS, lighter footprint. "Simplicity-first team" in a question
      stem points to Linkerd.

    🔑 COMMAND REFERENCE
    ────────────────────
    None yet — this chapter is conceptual. Your first real
    commands appear in the next hands-on chapter.

    🏋️ PRACTICE DRILL — Route Canary Traffic via Git
    ──────────────────────────────────────────────
    easypj-web has a DestinationRule defining v1 and v2 subsets. You need to
    shift traffic from 90/10 to 75/25 as part of a canary promotion step.
    TASK:
       Describe exactly which resource you'd edit, which field changes,
       and confirm this edit goes through the same PR-and-sync flow as any
       other GitOps change — no special mesh-specific process.

    📊 VISUAL SUMMARY
    ────────────────
    ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │ ▸ 3 mesh jobs                                                        │
    │   mTLS (encryption), traffic splitting (canary/A-B),                 │
    │   observability (latency/errors) — via Envoy or linkerd-proxy        │
    │   sidecars.                                                          │
    │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
    │ ▸ VirtualService vs DestinationRule                                  │
    │   VirtualService = routing rules + weights. DestinationRule =        │
    │   subset definitions + TLS/LB policy that VirtualService             │
    │   references.                                                        │
    │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
    │ ▸ Istio vs Linkerd                                                   │
    │   Istio = powerful, complex, many CRDs. Linkerd = simple,            │
    │   auto-mTLS, lighter footprint.                                      │
    │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
    │ ▸ Mesh config = just another CRD                                     │
    │   No special ArgoCD/Flux logic needed — VirtualServices sync         │
    │   through the same Application/Kustomization mechanism as            │
    │   everything else.                                                   │
    └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Chapter 23: GitOps for Infrastructure as Code
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    🔴 Advanced · ⏱️ ~1.5 hours · 🎯 CGOA: Practice & Security (20%) · 🏢 Daily-Work: Extending GitOps below the cluster

    WHY THIS CHAPTER MATTERS
    ────────────────────────
    Every chapter so far has managed what runs INSIDE Kubernetes — this
    chapter extends the same Git-as-source-of-truth idea to the cloud
    resources (databases, networks, buckets) the cluster depends on, closing
    the last gap between "GitOps for apps" and "GitOps for everything."

    🎯 LEARNING OBJECTIVES — by the end of this chapter you can:

         natively while Terraform's don't
      2. Provision a cloud resource (e.g. an RDS instance) as a Kubernetes
         CRD
      3. Describe what a Terraform Cloud Operator bridges
      4. Name the 3 layers of a full-stack GitOps repo and what belongs in
         each

    23.1 Infrastructure as Code (IaC) with GitOps
          - Terraform + GitOps: managing cloud infra
          - Crossplane: K8s-native infrastructure management
          - Pulumi + GitOps
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │         THREE PATHS FROM GIT TO PROVISIONED INFRASTRUCTURE                   │
         │                                                                              │
         │   Crossplane          Terraform (bridged)     Pulumi (bridged)               │
         │   ┌──────────────┐    ┌──────────────┐        ┌──────────────┐               │
         │   │ Composition + │    │ .tf files +   │        │ Pulumi prog +│             │
         │   │ Claim CRDs     │    │ Workspace CRD │        │ Workspace CRD│            │
         │   └───────┬────────┘    └───────┬───────┘        └───────┬───────┘           │
         │           │ ArgoCD applies      │ ArgoCD applies         │ ArgoCD applies    │
         │           │ directly            │ CRD only               │ CRD only          │
         │           ▼                     ▼                        ▼                   │
         │   ┌──────────────┐    ┌──────────────────┐    ┌──────────────────┐           │
         │   │ Crossplane     │    │ Terraform Cloud    │    │ Pulumi Kubernetes│       │
         │   │ provider       │    │ Operator/Atlantis  │    │ Operator          │      │
         │   │ (native K8s    │    │ runs terraform     │    │ runs pulumi up,   │      │
         │   │  reconciler)   │    │ apply, writes       │    │ writes status to  │     │
         │   └───────┬────────┘    │ status to the CRD  │    │ the CRD           │      │
         │           │             └─────────┬──────────┘    └─────────┬─────────┘      │
         │           ▼                       ▼                          ▼               │

          - What is Crossplane?
          - Managed resources as K8s CRDs

         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │ apiVersion: apiextensions.crossplane.io/v1                                   │

    23.3 Terraform + GitOps Integration
          - Terraform Cloud Operator
          - GitOps-driven Terraform runs
          - Managing cloud resources alongside K8s resources
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │ config repo (Git)                                                        │
         │   easypj-vpc/                                                            │
         │     main.tf, workspace.yaml   ──▶  ArgoCD applies ONLY workspace.yaml    │
         │                                               │                          │
         │                                               ▼                          │
         │                         ┌────────────────────────────────────┐           │
         │                         │ Terraform Cloud Operator             │         │
         │                         │ watches the Workspace CRD, runs      │         │
         │                         │ `terraform plan` + `apply` itself,   │         │
         │                         │ writes status/outputs back to the    │         │
         │                         │ CRD (which ArgoCD CAN see and diff)  │         │
         │                         └──────────────────┬───────────────────┘         │
         │                                            ▼                             │
         │                               AWS VPC, subnets, route tables             │
         │                                                                          │
         │ 💡 ArgoCD shows the Workspace CRD as Synced/Healthy, but never runs       │
         │    terraform itself — the Operator is doing that translation.            │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    23.4 The Full Stack GitOps Vision
          - Everything from Git: cloud → K8s → apps
          - One workflow to rule them all
          - Benefits and challenges of full-stack GitOps
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │                     fleet-config (Git) — ONE repo, 3 layers                  │
         │   ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐         │
         │   │ 01-infra/    VPC, EKS cluster, RDS   (Crossplane/Terraform)     │        │
         │   │ 02-platform/ ArgoCD, cert-manager,     (Helm/Kustomize)          │       │
         │   │              ingress-nginx                                      │        │
         │   │ 03-apps/     easypj-web Deployment,    (Kustomize overlays)      │       │
         │   │              Service, ConfigMap                                 │        │
         │   └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘         │
         │          │                  │                       │                        │
         │          ▼                  ▼                       ▼                        │
         │   Sync Wave -2         Sync Wave -1              Sync Wave 0                 │
         │   (cluster must        (platform must            (apps deploy                │
         │    exist first)         exist before apps)         last)                     │
         │                                                                              │
         │ 💡 App of Apps (Ch12) + Sync Waves order all 3 layers correctly from          │
         │    ONE root Application — a brand-new environment goes from an empty         │
         │    AWS account to a running easypj-web with a single `argocd app sync`.      │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    🔧 UNDER THE HOOD — Why Terraform Needs a Bridge and Crossplane Doesn't
    ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    Terraform's state lives in its own file (or remote backend) outside
    Kubernetes entirely — ArgoCD and Flux have no native concept of a
    .tfstate file, so there's nothing for their reconciliation loop to watch
    or diff directly. A Terraform Cloud Operator (or Atlantis) bridges this
    gap by wrapping a Terraform run inside a Kubernetes CRD (a Workspace
    resource) that DOES have status ArgoCD/Flux can observe, even though the
    actual apply still happens via Terraform's own engine, against
    Terraform's own state. Crossplane sidesteps this problem
    architecturally: it doesn't wrap another tool, it IS a Kubernetes
    controller — a Crossplane Instance resource's state lives in the K8s API
    itself (etcd), so it's natively watchable, diffable, and GitOps-syncable
    with zero bridge required.

    ⚠️ GOTCHAS
    ──────────
    • Assuming Terraform and Crossplane are interchangeable because both
      provision cloud infrastructure — the GitOps INTEGRATION story is
      fundamentally different (native CRD vs bridged state), even when the
      end result (an RDS instance exists) looks the same.
    • Deleting a Crossplane-managed resource via kubectl without
      understanding it deletes the REAL cloud resource too — Crossplane's
      whole point is that the CRD IS the source of truth for the cloud
      resource's lifecycle, deletion included.
    • Treating full-stack GitOps as "nice to have" complexity — the real
      payoff is PR review on infrastructure changes (a new RDS instance
      goes through the same review as a YAML change), which most teams
      currently skip entirely by clicking in a cloud console.

    ⚡ SPEED TIPS
    ─────────────
    • Crossplane = K8s-native (CRD, no bridge, ArgoCD/Flux manage it
      directly). Terraform = needs a bridge operator (TFC
      Operator/Atlantis) because its state lives outside K8s.
    • 3 layers of full-stack GitOps: infrastructure/ (cloud,
      Crossplane/Terraform) → clusters/ (K8s add-ons, policies) → apps/
      (Deployments, the stuff covered in Parts 1-5).
    • "Cloud resource represented as a Kubernetes CRD, deletable via
      kubectl" in a question = Crossplane, not Terraform.

    🔑 COMMAND REFERENCE
    ────────────────────
    None yet — this chapter is conceptual. Your first real
    commands appear in the next hands-on chapter.

    🏋️ PRACTICE DRILL — Pick the Bridge (or No Bridge)
    ────────────────────────────────────────────────
    easypj's team wants to provision a new S3-equivalent bucket for media
    uploads, managed the same GitOps way as everything else, with zero
    additional operational tooling to run and secure.
    TASK:
       Recommend Crossplane or Terraform+bridge for this specific
       constraint ("zero additional tooling"), and explain what extra
       component the other option would require running.

    📊 VISUAL SUMMARY
    ────────────────
    ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │ ▸ Crossplane = native                                                │
    │   Cloud resources as K8s CRDs — state lives in etcd, natively        │
    │   watchable/diffable/syncable by ArgoCD/Flux.                        │
    │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
    │ ▸ Terraform = bridged                                                │
    │   State lives outside K8s; a Terraform Cloud Operator or             │
    │   Atlantis wraps runs in a CRD so GitOps tools have something        │
    │   to watch.                                                          │
    │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
    │ ▸ 3-layer repo                                                       │
    │   infrastructure/ (cloud) → clusters/ (K8s add-ons) → apps/          │
    │   (workloads) — one repo, one workflow, one PR review process.       │
    │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
    │ ▸ Real payoff                                                        │
    │   Infrastructure changes get the same PR review as code — no         │
    │   more clicking in a cloud console unreviewed.                       │
    └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Chapter 24: GitOps Security Best Practices
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    🔴 Advanced · ⏱️ ~1.5 hours · 🎯 CGOA: Practice & Security (20%) · 🏢 Daily-Work: Hardening what you've built across Chapters 1-23

    WHY THIS CHAPTER MATTERS
    ────────────────────────
    Git being the deployment mechanism (Chapter 1's whole premise) also
    makes it the single biggest attack surface in your entire stack — this
    closing Part 6 chapter is the checklist that turns everything you've
    built into something you'd actually trust in production.

    🎯 LEARNING OBJECTIVES — by the end of this chapter you can:
    ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
      1. List the 4 layers of GitOps security defense-in-depth (repo,
         operator, secrets, compliance)
      2. Explain why signed commits matter specifically because Git IS the
         deployment mechanism
      3. Disable ArgoCD's default admin account in favor of SSO
      4. Map 3 compliance requirements (change management, audit trail,
         rollback) to the GitOps mechanism that satisfies each

    📍 YOU ARE HERE
    ────────────────
    Part 6: Advanced GitOps Patterns ▸ Chapter 4 of 4 ▸ Next: Part 7 — GitOps in Production (Chapter 25)

    24.1 Git Repository Security
          - Signed commits (GPG, SSH signing)
          - Branch protection and required reviews
          - CODEOWNERS for config repos
          - Detecting secrets in commits (git-secrets, truffleHog)
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │ # .github/CODEOWNERS in easypj-config                                        │
         │ # CODEOWNERS uses LAST-MATCH-WINS — put the catch-all FIRST, most            │
         │ # specific rule LAST, or a later broad pattern silently overrides it         │
         │ *                    @easypj-leads                                           │
         │ /overlays/staging/   @easypj-leads                                           │
         │ /overlays/prod/      @platform-team @easypj-leads                            │
         │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
         │ # git config for signed commits (per-developer, once)                        │
         │ $ git config --global commit.gpgsign true                                    │
         │ $ git config --global user.signingkey <your-GPG-key-ID>                      │
         │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
         │ # branch protection (GitHub repo settings, or via API/Terraform):            │
         │ #  - Require signed commits: ON                                              │
         │ #  - Require pull request reviews before merging: 2 approvals                │
         │ #  - Require review from Code Owners: ON                                     │
         │ #  - Require status checks (secret scan) to pass: ON                         │
         │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
         │ # pre-commit hook / CI step running a secret scanner:                        │
         │ $ trufflehog git file://. --since-commit HEAD~1 --fail                       │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │ LOCAL PRE-COMMIT SCAN              GITHUB PUSH PROTECTION (native)           │
         │ (git-secrets, truffleHog)          (secret scanning, org/repo setting)       │
         │ Runs on the DEVELOPER's machine    Runs SERVER-SIDE, before the push         │
         │   before the commit is even made     ever reaches the repository             │
         │ Can be skipped: --no-verify,       CANNOT be skipped silently — a            │
         │   or simply not installed            bypass requires selecting a             │
         │                                      reason (e.g. 'false positive'),         │
         │                                      which is itself logged                  │
         │ No organization-wide audit         Every bypass is recorded as an            │
         │   trail of who skipped it            alert, visible to org admins            │
         │                                                                              │
         │ 💡 These are complementary, not redundant — the same defense-in-              │
         │    depth idea as OPA/Gatekeeper backstopping PR review (Ch27): a             │
         │    local scanner catches mistakes early and cheaply, but GitHub's            │
         │    native push protection is the layer that can't be quietly                 │
         │    disabled by an individual developer.                                      │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    24.2 ArgoCD Security Hardening
          - TLS configuration
          - RBAC best practices
          - Network policies for ArgoCD components
          - Resource limits and isolation
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │ apiVersion: v1                                                               │
         │ kind: ConfigMap                                                              │
         │ metadata:                                                                    │
         │   name: argocd-cmd-params-cm                                                 │
         │   namespace: argocd                                                          │
         │ data:                                                                        │
         │   server.insecure: 'false'          # enforce TLS, no plaintext HTTP         │
         │   server.disable.auth: 'false'                                               │
         │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
         │ apiVersion: argoproj.io/v1alpha1                                             │
         │ kind: AppProject                                                             │
         │ metadata:                                                                    │
         │   name: easypj                                                               │
         │   namespace: argocd                                                          │
         │ spec:                                                                        │
         │   roles:                                                                     │
         │     - name: read-only                                                        │
         │       policies:                                                              │
         │         - p, proj:easypj:read-only, applications, get, easypj/*, allow       │
         │       groups:                                                                │
         │         - easypj-developers                                                  │
         │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
         │ apiVersion: networking.k8s.io/v1                                             │
         │ kind: NetworkPolicy                                                          │
         │ metadata:                                                                    │
         │   name: argocd-server-restrict                                               │
         │   namespace: argocd                                                          │
         │ spec:                                                                        │
         │   podSelector:                                                               │
         │     matchLabels:                                                             │
         │       app.kubernetes.io/name: argocd-server                                  │
         │   policyTypes: ['Ingress']                                                   │
         │   ingress:                                                                   │
         │     - from:                                                                  │
         │         - namespaceSelector:                                                 │
         │             matchLabels:                                                     │
         │               name: ingress-nginx                                            │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    24.3 Secret Rotation in GitOps
          - Automated secret rotation patterns
          - Graceful rotation without downtime
          - Reloader: auto-restart pods on secret change
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │ apiVersion: apps/v1                                                          │
         │ kind: Deployment                                                             │
         │ metadata:                                                                    │
         │   name: easypj-web                                                           │
         │   namespace: easypj                                                          │
         │   annotations:                                                               │
         │     reloader.stakater.com/auto: 'true'   # watch ALL referenced              │
         │                                           # Secrets/ConfigMaps               │
         │ spec:                                                                        │
         │   template:                                                                  │
         │     spec:                                                                    │
         │       containers:                                                            │
         │         - name: easypj                                                       │
         │           envFrom:                                                           │
         │             - secretRef:                                                     │
         │                 name: easypj-db-connection                                   │
         │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
         │ ROTATION FLOW:                                                               │
         │ 1. External Secrets Operator (Ch13) pulls a NEW database password            │
         │    from Vault/AWS Secrets Manager on its refresh interval                    │
         │ 2. The easypj-db-connection Secret's content changes in the cluster          │
         │ 3. Reloader detects the Secret changed, triggers a rolling restart           │
         │    of easypj-web — zero manual kubectl, zero downtime                        │
         │ 4. New pods start with the rotated credential already mounted                │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    24.4 Compliance and Audit
          - Meeting SOC2, HIPAA requirements with GitOps
          - Audit trails via Git history
          - Compliance-as-code patterns
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │ REQUIREMENT           │ GITOPS MECHANISM THAT SATISFIES IT                   │
         │ ───────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────           │
         │ Change management /    │ Every change is a PR — reviewed, approved,          │
         │   approval workflow     │ and merged before it can reach production          │
         │ ───────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────           │
         │ Audit trail (who/       │ Git commit history: author, timestamp,             │
         │   what/when)             │ signed commit, full diff — permanent              │
         │ ───────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────           │
         │ Ability to prove what   │ `git show <commit>` reproduces the EXACT           │
         │   was deployed, when     │ state deployed at any point in history            │
         │ ───────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────           │
         │ Rollback capability     │ `git revert` — a fast, auditable, provable         │
         │                         │ return to a known-good state (Ch31.7)              │
         │ ───────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────           │
         │ Least-privilege access  │ ArgoCD Projects + RBAC scope exactly which         │
         │   control                │ repos/namespaces/kinds each team can touch        │
         │ ───────────────────────┴──────────────────────────────────────────           │
         │ 💡 Auditors increasingly PREFER GitOps shops — the evidence for               │
         │    every one of these requirements already exists in Git, with no            │
         │    extra tooling or manual log-keeping required.                             │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    🔧 UNDER THE HOOD — Why This Chapter Is a Checklist, Not New Concepts
    ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    Every technique in this chapter is a callback: branch protection and
    CODEOWNERS from Chapter 6, Sealed Secrets/ESO/SOPS/Vault from Chapter
    13, RBAC and Projects from Chapter 9, Reloader for secret rotation.
    What's new here is the FRAME — thinking about all of it together as
    defense-in-depth layers (repo → operator → secrets → compliance) rather
    than as separate chapter topics. This matters because a real security
    review doesn't ask "did you read Chapter 13" — it asks "if an attacker
    compromises layer N, what stops them from reaching layer N+1," and
    that's a question only answerable once you see all 4 layers as one
    system.

    ⚠️ GOTCHAS
    ──────────
    • Treating any single layer as sufficient — signed commits stop
      impersonation but do nothing if the admin account still has a shared
      password; RBAC restricts blast radius but doesn't stop a leaked
      plaintext secret. Defense-in-depth means all 4 layers, not the
      strongest one.
    • Leaving ArgoCD's default admin account enabled after moving to SSO
      "just in case" — this defeats the entire hardening step; the
      disabled admin account should stay disabled.
    • Assuming compliance is a separate initiative from the GitOps
      practices already in place — Chapter 24.4's whole point is that
      change management, audit trail, and rollback compliance requirements
      are already satisfied by mechanisms from Chapters 1, 2, and 8 with
      zero extra tooling.
    • Assuming branch protection alone stops an unreviewed change from
      reaching the config repo — on GitHub, repository admins BYPASS
      branch protection rules by default, even with required reviews and
      signed commits enabled. The "Do not allow bypassing the above
      settings" option must be explicitly turned on, or an admin account
      compromise skips every rule in 24.1 entirely. GitHub's newer
      Rulesets mechanism fixes this more precisely than classic branch
      protection: bypass can be granted to a specific role, team, or
      GitHub App instead of an all-or-nothing admin exemption, and
      multiple Rulesets can layer on the same branch simultaneously.

    ⚡ SPEED TIPS
    ─────────────
    • 4 layers, memorize the order: Git repo (signed commits, branch
      protection, CODEOWNERS, pre-commit secret scanning) → Operator
      (disable default admin, SSO, RBAC, NetworkPolicy) → Secrets
      (rotation via ESO/Vault + Reloader) → Compliance (audit trail = git
      log, rollback = git revert).
    • "First step to harden ArgoCD" in a question is almost always disable
      default admin + enable SSO — it's the highest-leverage single action
      in this chapter.
    • Compliance-as-code shortcut: change management = PR review, audit
      trail = git log, rollback = git revert, separation of duties =
      CODEOWNERS. Four compliance asks, four GitOps mechanisms already
      covered.
    • If any CI workflow in Ch25 needs cloud credentials (e.g. a
      Terraform run), GitHub Actions OIDC lets the cloud provider issue a
      short-lived token per-run instead of storing a long-lived AWS/GCP
      key as a GitHub secret — the exact same "no long-lived credential
      sitting anywhere" principle as the pull model's security argument
      in Ch1.5, just applied to CI-to-cloud instead of CI-to-cluster.

    🔑 COMMAND REFERENCE
    ────────────────────
    ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │ git log --since=<date> --until=<date>                                            │
    │     Produce an instant, complete audit trail for any date range — the            │
    │     compliance answer to "show all changes."                                     │
    │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
    │ kubectl get pods -n argocd -l app.kubernetes.io/name=argocd-server               │
    │     Verify ArgoCD's own components before trusting any of its security           │
    │     config.                                                                      │
    └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    🏋️ PRACTICE DRILL — Map Compliance to Mechanism
    ─────────────────────────────────────────────
    A SOC2 auditor asks: "Show me your change management process, your audit
    trail, and your rollback procedure for production deployments."
    TASK:
       For each of the 3 asks, name the specific GitOps mechanism (from
       earlier chapters) that already answers it, with zero new tooling
       required.

    📊 VISUAL SUMMARY
    ────────────────
    ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │ ▸ 4-layer defense-in-depth                                           │
    │   Git repo → Operator (ArgoCD/Flux) → Secrets → Compliance —         │
    │   each layer assumes the ones below it can fail.                     │
    │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
    │ ▸ Highest-leverage first step                                        │
    │   Disable ArgoCD's default admin account, enable SSO — do this       │
    │   before anything else in this chapter.                              │
    │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
    │ ▸ Compliance-as-code                                                 │
    │   PR review = change management. git log = audit trail. git          │
    │   revert = rollback. Already built, just needs framing.              │
    │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
    │ ▸ Callback chapter                                                   │
    │   Every technique here was introduced earlier (Ch2, Ch6, Ch9,        │
    │   Ch13) — this chapter's value is the unified defense-in-depth       │
    │   frame.                                                             │
    └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
