    A.3  Git Commands for GitOps
         - git clone, commit, push, pull, revert, log, diff, tag
         [COMPLETE COMMAND REFERENCE TABLE]

         [GIT — essential cheat sheet for GitOps]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │ git clone <url>                                                                │
         │     Clone the config repo locally                                              │
         │ ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
         │ git commit -am "message"                                                       │
         │     Stage tracked changes + commit in one step                                 │
         │ ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
         │ git push origin main                                                           │
         │     Push commits — this is what triggers GitOps sync                           │
         │ ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
         │ git pull                                                                       │
         │     Fetch + merge the latest from the remote                                   │
         │ ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
         │ git revert <sha>                                                               │
         │     The GitOps-native rollback — undo commit, keeps history                    │
         │ ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
         │ git log --oneline -- <file>                                                    │
         │     Audit trail for one file                                                   │
         │ ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
         │ git diff HEAD~1 <file>                                                         │
         │     See exactly what the last commit changed                                   │
         │ ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
         │ git tag -a v1.2.3 -m "message"                                                 │
         │     Tag a release version                                                      │
         │ ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
         │ git show <sha>                                                                 │
         │     Full detail on a specific commit — who, what, when                         │
         │ ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
         │ git branch                                                                     │
         │     List local branches (should be short-lived, per Ch2/6)                     │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         - kubectl get, describe, logs, apply, delete, port-forward
         [COMPLETE COMMAND REFERENCE TABLE]

         [KUBECTL — essential cheat sheet for GitOps]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │ kubectl get all -n <namespace>                                                 │
         │     List every resource GitOps has deployed in a namespace                     │
         │ ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
         │ kubectl describe <kind> <name>                                                 │
         │     Full detail + recent events for one resource                               │
         │ ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
         │ kubectl logs deploy/<name>                                                     │
         │     Tail logs for a Deployment's pods                                          │
         │ ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │

         │     Manual apply — bootstrapping only, never routine use                       │
         │ ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
         │ kubectl apply -k <dir>                                                         │
         │     Render + apply a Kustomize overlay in one step                             │
         │ ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
         │ kubectl delete <kind> <name>                                                   │
         │     Manual delete — creates drift unless also removed from Git                 │
         │ ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
         │ kubectl port-forward svc/<name> 8080:443                                       │
         │     Reach a cluster-internal UI (e.g. ArgoCD) locally                          │
         │ ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
         │ kubectl get events -n <ns> --sort-by=.lastTimestamp                            │
         │     Recent cluster events, oldest first                                        │
         │ ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
         │ kubectl auth can-i <verb> <resource> --as=<sa>                                 │
         │     Check RBAC permissions for a service account                               │
         │ ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
         │ kubectl get secret <name> -o jsonpath="{.data.password}" | base64 -d           │
         │     Decode a Secret value (e.g. ArgoCD admin password)                         │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘


Appendix B: Complete easypj GitOps YAML Manifests
───────────────────────────────────────────────────
    B.1  Dockerfile for easypj
         - Multi-stage build for Django
         - Optimized for size and security
         [COMPLETE DOCKERFILE]

         [EASYPJ DOCKERFILE — multi-stage build for production]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  # Stage 1: Build dependencies                               │
         │  FROM python:3.12-slim AS builder                            │
         │  WORKDIR /app                                                │
         │  COPY requirements.txt .                                     │
         │  RUN pip install --no-cache-dir -r requirements.txt          │
         │                                                              │
         │  # Stage 2: Production image (smaller!)                      │
         │  FROM python:3.12-slim                                       │
         │  WORKDIR /app                                                │
         │  COPY --from=builder /usr/local/lib/python3.12/ /usr/local/  │
         │  COPY . .                                                    │
         │  RUN python manage.py collectstatic --noinput                │
         │  EXPOSE 8000                                                 │
         │  CMD ["gunicorn", "easypj.wsgi:application", "--bind",      │
         │       "0.0.0.0:8000", "--workers", "4"]                     │
         │                                                              │
         │  💡 Multi-stage: builder image ~500MB, final image ~200MB   │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    B.2  Kubernetes Manifests (base/)
         - deployment.yaml (easypj Django app)
         - service.yaml (ClusterIP for internal access)
         - configmap.yaml (Django settings)
         - secret.yaml (database credentials — SealedSecret)
         - ingress.yaml (external access via /jobs/)
         - pvc.yaml (persistent storage)
         - kustomization.yaml (base config)
         [COMPLETE YAML FILES]

         [EASYPJ BASE MANIFESTS — deployment, service, configmap, ingress, pvc, kustomization]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │ # ─── base/deployment.yaml ───                                                                   │
         │ apiVersion: apps/v1                                                                              │
         │ kind: Deployment                                                                                 │
         │ metadata:                                                                                        │
         │   name: easypj-web                                                                               │
         │   labels: { app: easypj }                                                                        │
         │ spec:                                                                                            │
         │   replicas: 3                                                                                    │
         │   selector: { matchLabels: { app: easypj } }                                                     │
         │   template:                                                                                      │
         │     metadata: { labels: { app: easypj } }                                                        │
         │     spec:                                                                                        │
         │       containers:                                                                                │
         │       - name: web                                                                                │
         │         image: ghcr.io/easypj/easypj:v1.0.0                                                      │
         │         ports: [{ containerPort: 8000 }]                                                         │
         │         envFrom:                                                                                 │
         │         - configMapRef: { name: easypj-config }                                                  │
         │         - secretRef: { name: easypj-db-credentials }                                             │
         │         resources:                                                                               │
         │           requests: { memory: "256Mi", cpu: "250m" }                                             │
         │           limits: { memory: "512Mi", cpu: "500m" }                                               │
         │         volumeMounts:                                                                            │
         │         - { name: media, mountPath: /app/media }                                                 │
         │       volumes:                                                                                   │
         │       - name: media                                                                              │
         │         persistentVolumeClaim: { claimName: easypj-media }                                       │
         │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
         │ # ─── base/service.yaml ───                                                                      │
         │ apiVersion: v1                                                                                   │
         │ kind: Service                                                                                    │
         │ metadata:                                                                                        │
         │   name: easypj-web                                                                               │
         │ spec:                                                                                            │
         │   type: ClusterIP                                                                                │
         │   selector: { app: easypj }                                                                      │
         │   ports: [{ port: 8000, targetPort: 8000 }]                                                      │
         │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
         │ # ─── base/configmap.yaml ───                                                                    │
         │ apiVersion: v1                                                                                   │
         │ kind: ConfigMap                                                                                  │
         │ metadata:                                                                                        │
         │   name: easypj-config                                                                            │
         │ data:                                                                                            │
         │   DJANGO_SETTINGS_MODULE: "easypj.settings.production"                                           │
         │   ALLOWED_HOSTS: "easypj.example.com"                                                            │
         │   DEBUG: "false"                                                                                 │
         │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
         │ # ─── base/secret.yaml — DO NOT commit plaintext! Use kubeseal (Ch13) ───                        │
         │ # kubeseal --scope cluster-wide -f secret.yaml -o yaml > sealed-secret.yaml                      │
         │ # The SealedSecret (not this file) is what actually lives in Git.                                │
         │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
         │ # ─── base/ingress.yaml ───                                                                      │
         │ apiVersion: networking.k8s.io/v1                                                                 │
         │ kind: Ingress                                                                                    │
         │ metadata:                                                                                        │
         │   name: easypj-web                                                                               │
         │   annotations: { kubernetes.io/ingress.class: "nginx" }                                          │
         │ spec:                                                                                            │
         │   rules:                                                                                         │
         │   - host: easypj.example.com                                                                     │
         │     http:                                                                                        │
         │       paths:                                                                                     │
         │       - path: /jobs                                                                              │
         │         pathType: Prefix                                                                         │
         │         backend:                                                                                 │
         │           service: { name: easypj-web, port: { number: 8000 } }                                  │
         │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
         │ # ─── base/pvc.yaml ───                                                                          │
         │ apiVersion: v1                                                                                   │
         │ kind: PersistentVolumeClaim                                                                      │
         │ metadata:                                                                                        │
         │   name: easypj-media                                                                             │
         │ spec:                                                                                            │
         │   accessModes: [ReadWriteOnce]                                                                   │
         │   resources: { requests: { storage: 10Gi } }                                                     │
         │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
         │ # ─── base/kustomization.yaml ───                                                                │
         │ apiVersion: kustomize.config.k8s.io/v1beta1                                                      │
         │ kind: Kustomization                                                                              │
         │ resources:                                                                                       │
         │   - deployment.yaml                                                                              │
         │   - service.yaml                                                                                 │
         │   - configmap.yaml                                                                               │
         │   - ingress.yaml                                                                                 │
         │   - pvc.yaml                                                                                     │
         │ images:                                                                                          │
         │   - name: easypj                                                                                 │
         │     newName: ghcr.io/easypj/easypj                                                               │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    B.4  ArgoCD Application YAML
         - easypj-app.yaml (complete Application CRD)
         - easypj-appset.yaml (ApplicationSet for environments)
         [COMPLETE YAML FILES]

         [ARGOCD APPLICATION + APPLICATIONSET]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │ # ─── easypj-app.yaml — single-environment Application ───                                       │
         │ apiVersion: argoproj.io/v1alpha1                                                                 │
         │ kind: Application                                                                                │
         │ metadata:                                                                                        │
         │   name: easypj-prod                                                                              │
         │   namespace: argocd                                                                              │
         │ spec:                                                                                            │
         │   project: default                                                                               │
         │   source:                                                                                        │
         │     repoURL: https://github.com/easypj/easypj-config.git                                         │
         │     targetRevision: main                                                                         │
         │     path: overlays/production                                                                    │
         │   destination:                                                                                   │
         │     server: https://kubernetes.default.svc                                                       │
         │     namespace: easypj-prod                                                                       │
         │   syncPolicy:                                                                                    │
         │     automated: { prune: true, selfHeal: true }                                                   │
         │     syncOptions: [CreateNamespace=true]                                                          │
         │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
         │ # ─── easypj-appset.yaml — one ApplicationSet, all 3 environments ───                            │
         │ apiVersion: argoproj.io/v1alpha1                                                                 │
         │ kind: ApplicationSet                                                                             │
         │ metadata:                                                                                        │
         │   name: easypj-environments                                                                      │
         │   namespace: argocd                                                                              │
         │ spec:                                                                                            │
         │   generators:                                                                                    │
         │     - list:                                                                                      │
         │         elements:                                                                                │
         │           - { env: dev,        namespace: easypj-dev }                                           │
         │           - { env: staging,    namespace: easypj-staging }                                       │
         │           - { env: production, namespace: easypj-prod }                                          │
         │   template:                                                                                      │
         │     metadata: { name: 'easypj-{{env}}' }                                                         │
         │     spec:                                                                                        │
         │       project: default                                                                           │
         │       source:                                                                                    │
         │         repoURL: https://github.com/easypj/easypj-config.git                                     │
         │         path: 'overlays/{{env}}'                                                                 │
         │       destination:                                                                               │
         │         server: https://kubernetes.default.svc                                                   │
         │         namespace: '{{namespace}}'                                                               │
         │       syncPolicy:                                                                                │
         │         automated: { prune: true, selfHeal: true }                                               │
         │         syncOptions: [CreateNamespace=true]                                                      │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    B.5  CI/CD Pipeline YAML
         - .github/workflows/ci.yaml (GitHub Actions)
         - .gitlab-ci.yml (GitLab CI alternative)
         [COMPLETE PIPELINE YAML]

         [CI/CD PIPELINES — GitHub Actions + GitLab CI]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │ # ─── .github/workflows/ci.yaml ───                                                              │
         │ name: CI — Build, Test & Update Config                                                           │
         │ on:                                                                                              │
         │   push: { tags: ['v*'] }                                                                         │
         │ jobs:                                                                                            │
         │   build-and-update:                                                                              │
         │     runs-on: ubuntu-latest                                                                       │
         │     steps:                                                                                       │
         │       - uses: actions/checkout@v4                                                                │
         │       - run: pip install flake8 && flake8 jobpost/                                               │
         │       - run: pip install -r requirements.txt && python manage.py test jobpost                    │
         │       - uses: docker/build-push-action@v5                                                        │
         │         with:                                                                                    │
         │           push: true                                                                             │
         │           tags: ghcr.io/easypj/easypj:${{ github.ref_name }}                                     │
         │       - uses: actions/checkout@v4                                                                │
         │         with:                                                                                    │
         │           repository: easypj/easypj-config                                                       │
         │           token: ${{ secrets.CONFIG_REPO_TOKEN }}                                                │
         │       - run: |                                                                                   │
         │           cd overlays/production                                                                 │
         │           kustomize edit set image ghcr.io/easypj/easypj:${{ github.ref_name }}                  │
         │           git config user.name "CI Bot"                                                          │
         │           git commit -am "CI: Update easypj to ${{ github.ref_name }}"                           │
         │           git push                                                                               │
         │ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
         │ # ─── .gitlab-ci.yml — GitLab CI alternative ───                                                 │
         │ stages: [test, build, update-config]                                                             │
         │ variables:                                                                                       │
         │   IMAGE: registry.gitlab.com/easypj/easypj                                                       │
         │                                                                                                  │
         │ test:                                                                                            │
         │   stage: test                                                                                    │
         │   script:                                                                                        │
         │     - pip install flake8 && flake8 jobpost/                                                      │
         │     - python manage.py test jobpost                                                              │
         │                                                                                                  │
         │ build:                                                                                           │
         │   stage: build                                                                                   │
         │   script:                                                                                        │
         │     - docker build -t $IMAGE:$CI_COMMIT_TAG .                                                    │
         │     - docker push $IMAGE:$CI_COMMIT_TAG                                                          │
         │   only: [tags]                                                                                   │
         │                                                                                                  │
         │ update-config:                                                                                   │
         │   stage: update-config                                                                           │
         │   script:                                                                                        │
         │     - git clone https://gitlab-ci-token:${CI_JOB_TOKEN}@gitlab.com/easypj/easypj-config.git      │
         │     - cd easypj-config/overlays/production                                                       │
         │     - kustomize edit set image $IMAGE:$CI_COMMIT_TAG                                             │
         │     - git commit -am "CI: Update to $CI_COMMIT_TAG" && git push                                  │
         │   only: [tags]                                                                                   │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘


Appendix C: GitOps Glossary — 100+ Terms
─────────────────────────────────────────
    [WHY THIS APPENDIX EXISTS] A novice hits unfamiliar vocabulary from
    ArgoCD, FluxCD, Kubernetes, and the wider cloud-native ecosystem all
    at once. Every 📖 Key Term box in the guide points back here.

    C.1  Core GitOps Terms (A-M)
    • Blue-Green — Deployment strategy running two complete environments with
      an instant traffic switch.
    • Canary — Deployment strategy gradually shifting traffic % with
      metric-based promotion/rollback.
    • CI/CD — Continuous Integration (build/test) + Continuous
      Delivery/Deployment.
    • Config Repo — Git repository containing declarative Kubernetes YAML —
      the single source of truth GitOps agents sync from.
    • Configuration as Code (CaC) — Storing a system's configuration as
      versioned text rather than manual changes; GitOps is CaC plus
      automated pull-based reconciliation on top (Ch32.1).
    • Continuous — In GitOps, describes the reconciliation loop
      specifically: it runs repeatedly and indefinitely once started, never
      a one-time event (Ch31.1).
    • CRD — Custom Resource Definition — extends the Kubernetes API with new
      resource types (e.g. Application, HelmRelease).
    • Crossplane — Kubernetes-native IaC tool managing cloud resources
      (databases, buckets) as CRDs.
    • Declarative — Describing the desired end state (WHAT you want) instead
      of the steps to get there (HOW) — the opposite of imperative.
    • DestinationRule — Istio CRD defining service subsets (v1/v2), load
      balancing, and TLS settings.
    • DevSecOps — Integrating security practices — PR review, signed
      commits, policy-as-code — throughout every delivery stage instead of
      as one final gate (Ch32.4).
    • DORA Metrics — Four industry-standard indicators of software delivery
      performance: deployment frequency, change failure rate, mean time to
      recovery, lead time for changes.
    • Drift — The difference between desired state (Git) and actual state
      (cluster) — what reconciliation detects and corrects.
    • eBPF — Extended Berkeley Packet Filter — kernel technology used by tools
      like Cilium for high-performance networking and observability.
    • ESO — External Secrets Operator — pulls secrets from cloud providers
      (AWS/GCP/Azure/Vault) at runtime; secrets never touch Git.
    • Event-Driven (Reconciliation) — A webhook or external event triggers
      immediate reconciliation instead of waiting for the next poll interval
      — the agent still pulls, only the trigger timing changes (Ch33.1).
    • External Reconciler — A reconciler architecture where the agent runs
      outside the cluster it manages, reaching in via an outbound-only
      connection — contrast with ArgoCD/Flux's default in-cluster pattern
      (Ch33.2).
    • Feedback Loop — The mechanism surfacing reconciliation status (synced,
      drifted, failed) back to humans and systems — notifications, alerts,
      and observability dashboards all implement it (Ch31.6).
    • GitOps — Managing infrastructure and applications using Git as the
      single source of truth, with declarative config and automated
      reconciliation.
    • GitOps Managed Software System — The complete set of resources whose
      desired state is declaratively described and continuously reconciled —
      defines the boundary of what GitOps actually controls (Ch31.4).
    • GitOps Maturity Model — A 4-level ladder (Git Trigger → Source of Truth
      → Full Reconciliation → Progressive Delivery) describing how far a
      team's GitOps practice has progressed. CGOA targets Level 3.
    • Grafana — Observability platform for metrics visualization, dashboards,
      and alerting — commonly paired with Prometheus.
    • Hub-and-Spoke — Multi-cluster architecture with one management (hub)
      cluster running ArgoCD/Flux and controlling many worker (spoke)
      clusters.
    • Hubble — Cilium's network observability tool, providing visibility into
      service mesh and eBPF-based networking traffic.
    • Imperative — Issuing step-by-step commands (e.g. kubectl scale) instead
      of declaring desired state in Git — the anti-pattern GitOps replaces.
    • Istio — Full-featured service mesh providing mTLS, fine-grained traffic
      splitting, and observability via Envoy sidecar proxies.
    • Kustomize — Kubernetes-native config management using a base + overlay
      pattern; built into kubectl, no templating language required.
    • Linkerd — Lightweight service mesh with automatic zero-config mTLS and a
      smaller resource footprint than Istio.
    • Loki — Log aggregation system — "Prometheus for logs" — commonly paired
      with Grafana for GitOps observability.
    • mTLS — Mutual TLS — automatic encryption of all pod-to-pod
      communication, provided by a service mesh.

    C.2  Core GitOps Terms (N-Z)
    • OPA / Gatekeeper — Policy engine for Kubernetes admission control —
      enforces policies (resource limits, approved registries) before
      deployment.
    • Progressive Delivery — Deploying gradually (canary, blue-green) with
      automated metric analysis and rollback, rather than all-at-once.
    • Prune — Sync option that deletes cluster resources which have been
      removed from Git — keeps the cluster clean but is destructive if
      misused.
    • Pull Model — The GitOps agent pulls desired state from Git, from inside
      the cluster — as opposed to the push model where CI pushes to the
      cluster.
    • Reconciliation — The continuous loop: Observe cluster state → Diff
      against Git → Act to correct any drift.
    • Reloader — Tool that auto-restarts pods when a referenced Secret or
      ConfigMap changes — enables zero-downtime credential rotation.
    • Sealed Secrets — Asymmetric encryption for Kubernetes Secrets — a public
      key encrypts on your laptop, only the cluster's private key can
      decrypt. Safe to commit to Git.
    • Self-Heal — Sync option where the agent auto-corrects drift — a manual
      change to the cluster is automatically reverted to match Git.
    • Semver — Semantic versioning (v1.2.3) — MAJOR.MINOR.PATCH — the
      recommended image-tagging strategy for GitOps.
    • Service Mesh — Infrastructure layer managing service-to-service
      communication: mTLS, traffic splitting, retries, and observability.
    • SOPS — Secrets OPerationS — encrypts individual YAML values with
      GPG/Age/KMS while keeping the file structure readable.
    • State Store — The durable, versioned location storing the Declarative
      Description that a reconciler reads from; Git is the default, OCI
      registries and object storage buckets are real alternatives (Ch31.5,
      Ch33.3).
    • Trunk-Based Development — Git branching strategy using short-lived
      feature branches merged frequently to main — the recommended pattern
      for GitOps (vs. GitFlow's long-lived branches).
    • Two-Repo Pattern — Separating application source code (app repo) from
      Kubernetes manifests (config repo) — different owners, different
      access controls.
    • Vault — HashiCorp's centralized secret management platform, offering
      dynamic credentials and full audit logging for enterprise compliance.
    • VirtualService — Istio CRD defining HTTP routing rules and
      traffic-splitting percentages between service subsets.

    C.3  ArgoCD-Specific Terms
    • App of Apps — Pattern where a root ArgoCD Application manages a folder
      of child Application YAMLs — one command bootstraps an entire cluster.
    • Application (ArgoCD) — CRD defining source (Git repo + path),
      destination (cluster + namespace), and sync policy.
    • ApplicationSet — ArgoCD CRD that auto-generates Applications from a
      template using generators (List, Cluster, Git, SCM, Matrix, Pull
      Request).
    • Auto-Sync — ArgoCD sync policy where changes are automatically applied
      without manual approval.
    • Cluster Generator — ApplicationSet generator that auto-creates one
      Application per registered/managed cluster.
    • Project (ArgoCD) — ArgoCD construct that groups Applications and
      constrains which source repos, destination clusters, and resource
      kinds are allowed — enables multi-tenant GitOps.
    • Rollout (Argo) — Argo Rollouts CRD that replaces the standard
      Deployment, adding canary and blue-green strategies with metric-based
      promotion.
    • Sync Wave — ArgoCD annotation controlling deployment order within a
      sync — lower numbers deploy first (e.g. database migration before app
      pods).

    C.4  FluxCD-Specific Terms
    • Bootstrap (Flux) — One command (flux bootstrap) that installs all Flux
      controllers, connects them to a Git repo, and pushes the initial
      config — Flux becomes self-managing.
    • Flagger — FluxCD's progressive delivery operator, using a Canary CRD
      integrated with service meshes (Istio, Linkerd) for automated traffic
      shifting.
    • FluxCD — CNCF-graduated GitOps operator using a micro-controller
      architecture — each concern (source, Kustomize, Helm, notifications,
      images) runs as an independent controller.
    • GitRepository — Flux CRD defining WHERE to pull configuration from (Git
      URL, branch/tag/semver, auth) — the Source Controller's input.
    • HelmRelease — Flux CRD for declaratively managing a Helm chart's full
      lifecycle: install, upgrade, rollback, and test.
    • HelmRepository — Flux CRD defining a Helm chart repository source
      (HTTP or OCI).
    • Image Automation — FluxCD feature that scans container registries,
      selects a version by policy, and commits the update back to Git — pure
      GitOps (contrast with ArgoCD's cluster-direct Image Updater).
    • ImagePolicy — Flux CRD selecting which image version to use, by semver
      range, alphabetical, numerical, or regex policy.
    • ImageRepository — Flux CRD that scans a container registry and records
      available image tags.
    • Kustomization (Flux) — Flux CRD defining WHAT to apply from a source —
      the main reconciliation engine, analogous to ArgoCD's Application.
    • Webhook Receiver — FluxCD component enabling near-instant reconciliation
      on a Git push event, instead of waiting for the next polling interval.


Appendix D: GitOps Troubleshooting Guide
─────────────────────────────────────────
    D.1  Common ArgoCD Issues (10 issues with solutions)
         - Application stuck in "Progressing"
         - Sync fails with "comparison error"
         - Out of sync but no changes visible
         - etc.

         1. SYMPTOM: Application stuck in "Progressing" indefinitely
            CAUSE: A health check never reports Healthy — a misconfigured readiness probe, or a Job that never completes.
            FIX: kubectl describe the resource; check probe config or the Job's backoffLimit and pod logs.

         2. SYMPTOM: Sync fails with "ComparisonError"
            CAUSE: A CRD referenced in the manifest isn't installed on the destination cluster yet.
            FIX: Install the CRD first (often via a lower sync-wave — see 11.5), then retry the sync.

         3. SYMPTOM: "OutOfSync" but argocd app diff shows no difference
            CAUSE: Another controller (an HPA, a mutating webhook) modifies a field after ArgoCD applies it.
            FIX: Add an ignoreDifferences entry for that specific field (see 8.4) — never disable selfHeal entirely.

         4. SYMPTOM: Application shows "Unknown" health/sync status
            CAUSE: Git repository unreachable — expired token, wrong URL, or a network/firewall block.
            FIX: argocd repo get <url> to check connectivity; rotate credentials; verify DNS and firewall rules.

         5. SYMPTOM: argocd login fails with "x509: certificate signed by unknown authority"
            CAUSE: ArgoCD server is using a self-signed certificate.
            FIX: Use --insecure for local testing only; for production, add the CA certificate properly instead.

         6. SYMPTOM: Sync succeeds but pods sit in "ImagePullBackOff"
            CAUSE: Wrong image tag or registry, or a missing imagePullSecret.
            FIX: Verify the tag exists in the registry; check the Deployment's imagePullSecrets field.

         7. SYMPTOM: prune: true deleted a resource you didn't expect
            CAUSE: A manifest was removed from Git — sometimes accidentally, e.g. a Kustomize path changed.
            FIX: git revert the removal if it was a mistake; review changes to prune-enabled paths more carefully before merging.

         8. SYMPTOM: ArgoCD UI unreachable after port-forward was working
            CAUSE: The port-forward process died — usually a closed terminal or SSH session.
            FIX: Restart the port-forward, or set up a persistent Ingress instead of relying on port-forward long-term.

         9. SYMPTOM: RBAC policy change has no effect
            CAUSE: Edited argocd-rbac-cm directly with kubectl (reverted by reconciliation) or a policy.csv line has a typo that silently never matches.
            FIX: Always edit RBAC via Git (Ch9); verify with argocd account can-i and check policy.csv syntax character-by-character.

         10. SYMPTOM: Reconciliation is very slow across many Applications
            CAUSE: A Redis cache issue, or the Application Controller is under-provisioned for the Application count.
            FIX: Restart argocd-redis first; if it recurs, increase Application Controller replicas/resources (see 7.1's Under the Hood).

    D.2  Common FluxCD Issues (10 issues with solutions)
         - Reconciliation failing
         - HelmRelease stuck
         - Image automation not triggering
         - etc.

         1. SYMPTOM: GitRepository shows Ready=False
            CAUSE: Wrong URL, the branch doesn't exist, or the auth secretRef is missing or wrong.
            FIX: flux get source git <name> for the exact error; verify URL, branch, and secretRef.

         2. SYMPTOM: Kustomization is Ready=True but nothing deployed
            CAUSE: The spec.path field points to an empty or incorrect directory in the source repo.
            FIX: Verify spec.path matches the actual directory containing your manifests.

         3. SYMPTOM: HelmRelease stuck "Installing"
            CAUSE: A chart error, or a required value is missing from spec.values.
            FIX: flux logs --kind=HelmRelease --name=<name>; check values against the chart's documented schema.

         4. SYMPTOM: Image automation never commits an update
            CAUSE: Missing the $imagepolicy marker comment in the YAML — Flux has no anchor to know where to write.
            FIX: Add the marker comment (e.g. # {"$imagepolicy": "flux-system:policy-name"}) next to the image field (see Ch18 Under the Hood).

         5. SYMPTOM: ImagePolicy selects an unexpected version
            CAUSE: The semver range is too permissive, or pre-release tags are unintentionally included.
            FIX: Tighten the semver range; double-check whether the policy type is semver, alphabetical, numerical, or regex.

         6. SYMPTOM: Flux controllers crash-looping
            CAUSE: Resource limits set too low, or missing RBAC for a controller's ServiceAccount.
            FIX: kubectl describe pod -n flux-system; look for OOMKilled or Forbidden events specifically.

         7. SYMPTOM: Notification never arrives despite an Alert being configured
            CAUSE: The Provider's webhook URL is wrong, or the Alert's eventSources list doesn't include the resource that actually changed.
            FIX: Verify the Provider's secret/URL; check the Alert's eventSources against the resource kind and name.

         8. SYMPTOM: Webhook receiver never triggers instant reconciliation
            CAUSE: Webhook secret mismatch between the Git provider and Flux's Receiver, or a firewall blocking inbound traffic.
            FIX: Regenerate and match the secret on both sides; verify the receiver's ingress path is reachable from the Git provider.

         9. SYMPTOM: flux reconcile <kustomization> command hangs
            CAUSE: The source (GitRepository) itself isn't Ready, so there's nothing new to reconcile against.
            FIX: Fix the source first with flux get source git, then retry the reconcile.

         10. SYMPTOM: HelmRelease upgrade fails and doesn't auto-rollback
            CAUSE: upgrade.remediation.remediateLastFailure is not set to true.
            FIX: Add upgrade.remediation.remediateLastFailure: true to the HelmRelease spec (see Ch17 Gotchas).

    D.3  General GitOps Issues
         - Git authentication failures
         - YAML validation errors
         - Namespace conflicts
         [TROUBLESHOOTING FLOWCHARTS]

         [GITOPS TROUBLESHOOTING — decision tree]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  APP NOT SYNCING?                                             │
         │  ├── Check ArgoCD UI: What's the error?                      │
         │  │   ├── "Repository not found" → Check repo URL + creds    │
         │  │   ├── "Permission denied" → Check RBAC + namespace       │
         │  │   ├── "Invalid YAML" → Check manifest syntax              │
         │  │   ├── "ImagePullBackOff" → Check registry + image tag    │
         │  │   ├── "CrashLoopBackOff" → Check app logs                 │
         │  │   └── "OutOfSync" but no diff → Check ignoreDifferences  │
         │  │                                                           │
         │  APP SHOWS HEALTHY BUT NOT WORKING?                          │
         │  ├── kubectl port-forward to pod → test directly             │
         │  ├── Check Service selector matches pod labels               │
         │  ├── Check Ingress hostname + path rules                     │
         │  └── Check NetworkPolicy isn't blocking traffic              │
         │                                                              │
         │  RECONCILIATION LOOP STUCK?                                   │
         │  ├── Check argocd-application-controller logs                │
         │  ├── Too many apps? Increase controller replicas             │
         │  ├── Redis cache issue? Restart argocd-redis                 │
         │  └── Git repo unreachable? Check network + credentials       │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    E.1  Official Documentation Links
         • ArgoCD — argo-cd.readthedocs.io
         • FluxCD — fluxcd.io/docs
         • OpenGitOps (CNCF GitOps principles) — opengitops.dev
         • Kubernetes — kubernetes.io/docs
         • Kustomize — kubectl.docs.kubernetes.io/references/kustomize
         • Helm — helm.sh/docs
         • Argo Rollouts — argo-rollouts.readthedocs.io
         • Flagger — docs.flagger.app
         • Sealed Secrets — github.com/bitnami-labs/sealed-secrets
         • External Secrets Operator — external-secrets.io
         • SOPS — github.com/getsops/sops
         • Crossplane — docs.crossplane.io

    E.2  Community Resources (Slack, Forums, GitHub)
         • CNCF Slack — slack.cncf.io — join #argo-cd and #flux channels
         • ArgoCD GitHub Discussions — github.com/argoproj/argo-cd/discussions
         • FluxCD GitHub Discussions — github.com/fluxcd/flux2/discussions
         • ArgoCon and GitOpsCon — CNCF-affiliated conference talks, many free on YouTube
         • r/kubernetes — active community troubleshooting threads, searchable history
         • Stack Overflow tags: argocd, flux-cd, kubernetes — good for specific error messages

    E.3  Recommended Books and Courses
         • "GitOps and Kubernetes" (Manning) — Billy Yuen, Alexander Matyushentsev, Todd Ekenstam, Jesse Suen
         • "Kubernetes Up & Running" (O'Reilly) — Kelsey Hightower, Brendan Burns, Joe Beda — strong K8s prerequisite reading for Chapter 3
         • Linux Foundation's official CGOA exam prep course (training.linuxfoundation.org) — pairs directly with this guide's Part 8
         • ArgoCD and FluxCD official "Getting Started" tutorials — best for hands-on practice alongside Chapter 5/16
         • CNCF's free GitOps Working Group webinars — cover the same principles as Chapter 4, presented by the standard's authors

    E.4  Certification Resources (CGOA, CKA, CKAD)
         • CGOA (Certified GitOps Associate) — training.linuxfoundation.org, the certification this guide targets
         • CKA (Certified Kubernetes Administrator) — recommended BEFORE or alongside CGOA; Chapter 3's K8s essentials assume roughly this level
         • CKAD (Certified Kubernetes Application Developer) — complements CGOA well if your role is more application-focused than cluster-operations-focused
         • CKS (Certified Kubernetes Security Specialist) — a natural next step after CGOA for teams going deep on Chapter 24's security patterns
         • killer.sh / KillerCoda — scenario-based practice environments useful for any of the above, not just written practice questions

──────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    F.1  Section 1: GitOps Fundamentals (30 questions)
    F.2  Section 2: ArgoCD (30 questions)
    F.3  Section 3: FluxCD (20 questions)

    F.5  Answer Key with Explanations

    [F.1 SAMPLE QUESTIONS — GitOps Fundamentals]
         30 questions (full domain is 30% of the real exam):

         Q1. Which of the following is NOT one of the 4 core GitOps principles?
              1. Declarative configuration
              2. Versioned and immutable
              3. Pushed automatically by CI
              4. Continuously reconciled

         Q2. In the reconciliation loop, what happens during the "Diff" step?
              1. The agent pulls the latest code from Git
              2. The agent compares desired state (Git) with actual state (cluster)
              3. The agent deploys new pods
              4. The agent sends a Slack notification

         Q3. Why is the pull-based model more secure than push-based CI/CD?
              1. It uses HTTPS instead of SSH
              2. Cluster credentials never leave the cluster — the agent runs inside it
              3. It encrypts all network traffic automatically
              4. It requires two-factor authentication

         Q4. A team has enabled automated sync with prune:true and selfHeal:true. Which GitOps Maturity Level have they reached?
              1. Level 1 — Git Trigger
              2. Level 2 — Source of Truth
              3. Level 3 — Full Reconciliation
              4. Level 4 — Progressive Delivery

         Q5. Which repository pattern is considered a GitOps anti-pattern?
              1. Directory-based environments (base/ + overlays/)
              2. Separate app and config repos
              3. Long-lived environment branches (dev/staging/prod branches)
              4. Trunk-Based Development

         Q6. Why does GitOps recommend Trunk-Based Development over GitFlow?
              1. GitFlow doesn't support Kubernetes
              2. Trunk-Based Development's short-lived branches keep main always deployable, minimizing drift
              3. GitFlow requires a paid Git host
              4. Trunk-Based Development eliminates the need for pull requests

         Q7. What is the "config repo" in the two-repo GitOps pattern used for?
              1. Application source code
              2. Kubernetes declarative YAML manifests
              3. CI pipeline scripts only
              4. Docker Hub credentials

         Q8. In the thermostat analogy for GitOps, what does "the thermostat constantly checking the room temperature" represent?
              1. A one-time deployment
              2. The continuous reconciliation loop
              3. A manual kubectl apply
              4. The CI pipeline

         Q9. Which Git concept lets you see exactly WHO changed WHAT and WHEN in the config repo?
              1. .gitignore
              2. Commit history
              3. A merge conflict
              4. A detached HEAD

         Q10. Why does GitOps recommend signed commits specifically?
              1. To make commits load faster
              2. To cryptographically prove who authored a change and that it wasn't tampered with, since Git itself is the deployment trigger
              3. To compress the repository size
              4. To bypass code review requirements

         Q11. Which is the correct definition of "Desired State" as distinct from "Declarative Description"?
              1. Desired State is the YAML file; Declarative Description is what it means
              2. Desired State is the abstract target the description represents; Declarative Description is the written artifact itself
              3. They are exactly the same concept with two names
              4. Desired State only applies to ArgoCD; Declarative Description only applies to FluxCD

         Q12. A manual-sync-only Application shows "OutOfSync" for 3 hours before an engineer manually triggers a sync. During those 3 hours, which is true?
              1. Neither drift detection nor reconciliation has occurred
              2. Drift has been detected but not yet reconciled
              3. Drift has been both detected and reconciled
              4. This scenario is impossible in GitOps

         Q13. Which of these is NOT part of the "GitOps Managed Software System" for an app, assuming no infrastructure tool like Crossplane is in use?
              1. The application's Deployment manifest
              2. The application's ConfigMap
              3. A cloud IAM policy changed by hand in the provider's web console
              4. A Sealed Secret decrypted by the cluster

         Q14. What is the single defining requirement that separates a "State Store" from a plain file share?
              1. It must be free to use
              2. It must be durable, versioned, and ideally provide an audit trail
              3. It must run on AWS specifically
              4. It must use YAML exclusively

         Q15. Which THREE guide chapters, taken together, all implement what Chapter 31 formally names the "Feedback Loop"?
              1. Ch1, Ch2, Ch3
              2. Ch11, Ch19, Ch26
              3. Ch7, Ch16, Ch25
              4. Ch13, Ch14, Ch15

         Q16. Why is tool-native rollback (e.g. argocd app rollback) considered less durable than a git revert?
              1. It's slower
              2. It changes only the live cluster; Git still shows the newer commit, so a future reconciliation could re-apply the reverted change
              3. It requires a Kubernetes upgrade
              4. It only works with FluxCD

         Q17. GitOps's "Pulled Automatically" principle is satisfied by which architectural detail?
              1. The CI pipeline holds a kubeconfig and applies changes directly
              2. An agent running INSIDE the cluster fetches changes itself; no external system pushes credentials in
              3. A developer manually runs kubectl apply after every merge
              4. The cluster administrator emails the YAML to the on-call engineer

         Q18. In the GitOps Maturity Model, what specifically distinguishes Level 3 (Full Reconciliation) from Level 2 (Source of Truth)?
              1. Level 3 adds a web UI
              2. Level 3 adds automated sync WITH prune and selfHeal, not just Git as the source of truth
              3. Level 3 requires a paid support contract
              4. Level 3 removes the need for Git entirely

         Q19. Why are long-lived environment branches (dev/staging/prod) a GitOps anti-pattern compared to directory-based overlays on one branch?
              1. Git doesn't support multiple branches
              2. They require manual cherry-picking/merging between branches, reintroducing the drift problem GitOps exists to eliminate
              3. They make the repository too large
              4. They're incompatible with FluxCD specifically

         Q20. What is the primary security advantage of the pull model over traditional push-based CI/CD?
              1. Pull is faster
              2. Cluster credentials never need to leave the cluster, since the agent runs inside it and initiates the connection itself
              3. Pull uses less network bandwidth
              4. Pull eliminates the need for a Git repository

         Q21. A Deployment YAML sets "replicas: 3." What term does Chapter 31 use for the written YAML file itself, as opposed to the abstract target it represents?
              1. Desired State
              2. Declarative Description
              3. State Store
              4. Feedback Loop

         Q22. Which statement correctly distinguishes Continuous Delivery from Continuous Deployment?
              1. They are identical practices
              2. Continuous Delivery packages every passing change into a release a human must still decide to deploy; Continuous Deployment removes that human gate entirely
              3. Continuous Delivery only applies to databases
              4. Continuous Deployment is a manual process

         Q23. Why does GitOps NOT strictly require Continuous Deployment (fully automated, no human gate)?
              1. GitOps is incompatible with automation
              2. A team can run at GitOps Maturity Level 2 with manual sync and still be practicing GitOps — they've simply chosen a human approval gate
              3. Kubernetes forbids automated deployment
              4. Continuous Deployment requires a different version control system

         Q24. What does the Two-Repo Pattern separate, and why?
              1. Frontend code from backend code, for performance
              2. Application source code from Kubernetes manifests, so developers and operators can have different ownership and access controls
              3. Test code from production code, to save disk space
              4. Public repos from private repos, for licensing reasons

         Q25. In the CGOA sense, what is the boundary test for whether a resource is part of the "GitOps Managed Software System"?
              1. Whether it costs money to run
              2. Whether a Declarative Description exists for it AND it is actively reconciled
              3. Whether it is written in YAML
              4. Whether it runs on Kubernetes at all, regardless of how it got there

         Q26. Which of the following would GitOps's "Versioned and Immutable" principle flag as a violation?
              1. Committing a new image tag to Git and letting the agent apply it
              2. Tagging a production image as :latest, which silently changes meaning over time
              3. Using git revert to undo a bad change
              4. Storing YAML manifests in a Git repository

         Q27. What is Kubernetes' role relative to GitOps, most precisely?
              1. Kubernetes IS GitOps
              2. Kubernetes is the target platform GitOps agents reconcile against; GitOps is a deployment practice, not a Kubernetes feature
              3. GitOps replaces the Kubernetes API server
              4. Kubernetes requires GitOps to function at all

         Q28. A teammate SSHes into a node and runs kubectl edit deployment to fix an outage. What happens at the next reconciliation cycle if Git was never updated?
              1. Nothing — manual changes are always preserved
              2. The GitOps agent detects the drift and, if selfHeal is enabled, reverts the manual change back to match Git
              3. The cluster deletes the Deployment entirely
              4. ArgoCD automatically updates Git to match the manual change

         Q29. Why is "Versioned and Immutable" considered a GitOps principle rather than just a Git feature?
              1. It's not actually a principle
              2. It guarantees every past desired state is recoverable and no state was overwritten in place, which is what makes reliable rollback and audit possible
              3. It only matters for compliance audits, not technical operation
              4. It applies only to container images, never to YAML

         Q30. Which single sentence best distinguishes "State Drift" from "State Reconciliation"?
              1. They are synonyms
              2. Drift is the measured difference between desired and actual state; Reconciliation is the corrective action that resolves it
              3. Drift only happens in FluxCD; Reconciliation only happens in ArgoCD
              4. Drift is detected by humans; Reconciliation is detected by machines

         Q1. Which ArgoCD component is responsible for cloning Git repositories?
              1. API Server
              2. Application Controller
              3. Repository Server
              4. Redis Cache

         Q2. What does prune: true do in an ArgoCD sync policy?
              1. Deletes resources that have been removed from Git
              2. Prevents any deletions from ever happening

         Q3. Which ArgoCD construct constrains which source repos, destination clusters, and resource kinds a team's Applications may use?

              3. RBAC policy

              1. argocd app sync
              2. argocd app get
              3. argocd app diff
              4. argocd app list

         Q5. What is the key difference between argocd app rollback and git revert?
              1. They are functionally identical
              2. argocd app rollback changes only the live cluster; git revert also updates Git, so the change is permanent and auditable
              3. git revert only works with FluxCD, not ArgoCD
              4. argocd app rollback requires cluster-admin, git revert does not

         Q6. You need to deploy easypj identically to every cluster labeled env=prod, including clusters registered in the future. Which ApplicationSet generator fits best?

              2. Runs the reconciliation loop: watches, diffs, and applies changes
              3. Serves the web dashboard
              4. Caches manifests for performance

         Q9. What is Redis used for in ArgoCD's architecture?

         Q10. Which sync option automatically deletes cluster resources that have been removed from Git?

              4. retry

         Q11. Which sync option automatically reverts a manual change made directly to the cluster?
              1. prune
              2. selfHeal
              3. syncOptions
              4. finalizers

         Q12. What does argocd app diff do?
              1. Applies pending changes immediately
              2. Shows exactly what would change BEFORE triggering a sync — the safety net before a production sync
              3. Deletes the Application
              4. Compares two different clusters

         Q13. What is the purpose of an ArgoCD Project?
              1. To organize documentation only
              2. To constrain which source repos, destination clusters, and resource kinds an Application may use — enabling multi-tenant GitOps
              3. To replace the need for RBAC
              4. To store container images

         Q14. Which ApplicationSet generator automatically creates one Application per matching cluster, including clusters registered in the future?
              1. List generator
              2. Git directory generator
              3. Cluster generator with a label selector
              4. Pull Request generator

         Q15. What is the "App of Apps" pattern?
